# CRLS Rowing — Official Website Research Dossier

**Source:** https://crlsrowing.org/ · **Scraped:** 2026-09-05 · **Method:** complete WordPress REST API dump (216 posts, 59 pages, 18 categories, 11 tags, 3 author accounts) plus Wayback Machine CDX enumeration and retrieval of 20 removed/older pages.

> **Accuracy note.** Everything below is quoted or paraphrased from crlsrowing.org or its Wayback snapshots. Where the site does not state something, this document says **UNKNOWN**. Nothing has been inferred, guessed or embellished. Where the site contradicts itself, both versions are given.

---

## 1. Program history

### 1.1 Verbatim text of the current *Our History* page

<https://crlsrowing.org/about/history-of-the-program/>

> Formed in 1643, the Cambridge Latin School was the second public high school in the not yet named United States. Located in Cambridge, Massachusetts, it was formed shortly after the Boston Latin School was established across the Charles River. For more on the history of CRLS, check out The Cambridge Rindge and Latin School: Yesterday and Today, a book by John Langome.
>
> The Cambridge Latin High Crew raced from 1889 until the stock market crash of 1929 ended the program. A small program, the team raced in fours against rivals Rindge Technical School and Brown & Nichols.
>
> The program resurfaced in 1989 as the Cambridge Rindge & Latin Crew Club (Cambridge High & Latin and The Rindge Technical School merged in 1978) and began rowing with the Community Rowing program in Boston. While the club often fielded complete eights, the team rowed under the name and colors of Community Rowing. Driven to practice by teachers/coaches Linda Lipkin, Phyllis Bretholtz, and Tobe Korsgren the Club was enthusiastically supported by Athletic Director Bill Bates and it was their combined efforts that led to the inclusion of a CRLS boat bay in the remodeled Cambridge Boat Club in 1999.
>
> Thus, Spring 2000 was the first official season of the CRLS crew under its own name and colors. The season began that January as a part-time, non-racing program with 12 students with little rowing experience and no equipment. By March, it had exploded into a six-day-a-week varsity program with 50 kids, five boats, five races, and two coaches. The rest, as they say, is history.

### 1.2 Timeline of dated facts

| Year | Event |
|---|---|
| **1643** | Cambridge Latin School formed; described on the site as the second public high school in the not-yet-named United States. Formed shortly after Boston Latin School was established across the Charles River. |
| **1889-1929** | Cambridge Latin High Crew raced. A small program racing in fours against rivals Rindge Technical School and Brown & Nichols. The stock market crash of 1929 ended the program. |
| **1978** | Cambridge High & Latin and The Rindge Technical School merged. |
| **1989** | Program resurfaced as the Cambridge Rindge & Latin Crew Club; began rowing with the Community Rowing program in Boston, under the name and colors of Community Rowing. Often fielded complete eights. |
| **1999** | A CRLS boat bay was included in the remodeled Cambridge Boat Club, resulting from the combined efforts of teachers/coaches Linda Lipkin, Phyllis Bretholtz and Tobe Korsgren, with the enthusiastic support of Athletic Director Bill Bates. |
| **January 2000** | Season began as a part-time, non-racing program with 12 students with little rowing experience and no equipment. |
| **March 2000** | Had grown into a six-day-a-week varsity program with 50 kids, five boats, five races, and two coaches. |
| **Spring 2000** | First official season of CRLS crew under its own name and colors. |
| **2003-2004** | Earliest MPSRA championship season listed in the archived Championships table (Fall 2003 / Spring 2004 era). |
| **2004** | First year of the Greater Boston League championships listing for CRLS girls and boys (per archived Championships page: '2004 – First Year'). |
| **2014 (June 7-8)** | Sent three boats to National Scholastic Championships, Saratoga Springs, NY; Boys 1st Varsity Lightweight 4+ took SILVER. Post states the team 'made history'. |
| **2017 (June 9-11)** | Boys Lightweight 4+ raced USRowing Youth National Championships in Sarasota, FL, as the fastest Boys Lightweight 4+ representing the Northeast Region; finished 4th in the B Final = 10th fastest Boys Lightweight 4+ in the country. |
| **2022 (May)** | Girls 1V4+ won gold at the USRowing Northeast Youth Regional Championships — the first-ever CRLS girls' crew, and first CRLS Varsity 4+, to qualify for Nationals. |
| **2023 (October)** | Board post notes 'The program also has a major anniversary coming up' (anniversary not named on the site — UNKNOWN which). |
| **2024 (May 25-26)** | 'Historic Weekend': first time ALL 4 Boys Crews and ALL 8 CRLS Crews qualified for the NEIRA Championship Regatta; first CRLS crew to finish 1st in a NEIRA final (Girls 1V, Petite Final). At MPSRA the Boys Varsity 4+ took the title of STATE CHAMPIONS. |
| **2026 (May)** | Record-breaking spring season; boys' and girls' first varsity fours both qualified for Youth Nationals in Sarasota — described as 'the second girls' boat and fourth boys' boat to qualify for Nationals from CRLS and the first time ever sending two boats at once'. |

### 1.3 History text removed from the live site (recovered via Wayback)

- An older version of the About/History 'Today...' section (archived 2014-12-23) said the team competed in THREE leagues, including the Dual County League. The live site now lists only MPSRA and NEIRA.
- Archived at: https://web.archive.org/web/20141223/http://crlsrowing.org/about/history-of-the-program
- The archived **Championships** page states the Mayor's Cup is a *"Revival of a century old race. CRLS and BB&N used to race each other from the end of the 1800's through 1929 when Cambridge High and Latin stopped having a team."*
- The archived **Championships** page gives founding dates that **conflict** with the live History page: *"The two oldest public high schools in the United States. Boston Latin School was founded in 1638 and the Cambridge Latin School was founded in 1641."* The live History page says Cambridge Latin School was *formed in 1643*. **Both readings exist on the site; the discrepancy is unresolved.**

## 3. Program facts

- **Identity:** CRLS Crew / CRLS Rowing — a sport at Cambridge Rindge & Latin School (CRLS), the district public high school in Cambridge, MA.
- **Tagline:** At Home on the Charles (site title: 'CRLS Rowing – At Home on the Charles'). Older versions of the site used the strapline 'Home on the Charles'.
- **No-fee program:** 'We are a no-fee, competitive public school rowing program that removes financial barriers and makes the sport of rowing accessible to all interested students.'
- **Supported by:** Cambridge Rindge and Latin School, the City of Cambridge, the Cambridge Boat Club, and the generosity of private donors and community supporters.
- **Size:** Approximately 90 student-athletes each season ('Today, we proudly serve approximately 90 student-athletes each season'). The Coaches-Join-Us page says 'Our team of 90+ athletes'. A 2025 post calls it 'the largest team at CRLS'. An archived Boat Fund page (c.2014) said 'We now have more than 80 student athletes'.
- **Season structure:** Club sport in the Fall; varsity sport in the Spring. Separate boys and girls teams.
- **Leagues:** Massachusetts Public School Rowing Association (MPSRA) — mpsra.org; New England Interscholastic Rowing Association (NEIRA) — neirarowing.org
- **Leagues (historical):** An archived 2014 version of the page listed THREE leagues, adding the Dual County League. Archived Championships page also tracks a Greater Boston League (GBL) championship series for boys and girls from 2004 through 2012.
- **Home venue:** Cambridge Boat Club (CBC), 2 Gerry's Landing Road, Cambridge MA 02138, on the Charles River. CBC was incorporated under the laws of the Commonwealth of Massachusetts on April 7, 1909; the original clubhouse site was on the north bank of the Charles near the foot of Hawthorne Street in Cambridge; the Club is now located on Gerry's Landing.
- **Other practice locations:** Russell Field (indoor/land practice); War Memorial Recreation Center (swim tests); Harvard Stadium (stadium running); Newell Boathouse and St. Ann's referenced in older-season posts
- **Parent group:** Association for the Support of Cambridge Crew (ASCC), also styled 'CRLS Crew Parent Board'. Organized under the non-profit Friends of Cambridge Athletics (FOCA).
- **Non-profit status:** CRLS Crew is a non-profit organization under the umbrella of the Friends of Cambridge Athletics. Donations tax deductible under Federal 501(c)(3). EIN: 04-3480847.
- **Mailing address:** CRLS Crew, P.O. Box 400116, Cambridge, MA 02140
- **Suggested contributions:** Voluntary contribution of $75–$150 per athlete suggested (2026). Regatta food funding asks: $50 per novice, $100 per varsity athlete. · Venmo @CRLS-Rowing
- **Social:** https://www.instagram.com/crlscrew/ (handle: crlscrew, 'Cambridge Rindge & Latin School Rowing'). Historic Twitter handle @FalconRowing appeared in the archived site footer.
- **ASCC President:** Isabella (Izzy) Ehrlich, President, Association for the Support of CRLS Crew (ASCC) — signature on a 2025 post

**Key email addresses**

- `coaches`: crls@cambridge-boat-club.org
- `parent_board`: crlsrowingteam@gmail.com
- `treasurer`: treasurercrlscrew@gmail.com
- `historic_generic`: questions@crlsrowing.org / boatfund@crlsrowing.org / auctions@crlsrowing.org (archived, c.2012-2014)

**Parent Board, Academic Year 2027**

| Name | Role | Email |
|---|---|---|
| Shannon Finley | Communications & Maintenance (helping with boats and facilities) | shannonfm@hotmail.com |
| Carroll Magill | Secretary and Co-Chair, Regatta & Race Operations | carrollmagill@gmail.com |
| Marcela Millan | Treasurer | treasurercrlscrew@gmail.com |

Board description (verbatim): *"We are the Association for the Support of Cambridge Crew (CRLS Crew Parent Board). We are organized to provide support for the team at events and to help raise money for team expenses. Our board consists primarily of parents of active members of the crew team. We manage communication to families, meet monthly with the team coaches to help coordinate team needs such as fundraisers, regatta and race food and transportation, social events and management of the team's uniforms. Working with the Friends of Cambridge Athletics (FOCA), we raise operating and equipment funds for the crew team. We appreciate the support of the CRLS Athletic Department and our generous community to help us continue to offer the chance to row to any student at CRLS."*

## 4. Boats, shells, equipment and the fleet

> **Bottom line: the site publishes no fleet inventory and names no individual shells.** `named_shells: UNKNOWN`. Everything the site does say about equipment is collected below.

- 1999: a CRLS boat bay was included in the remodeled Cambridge Boat Club. The team's boats live in that bay at CBC; 2012 post refers to snacks served 'in the back of the boat bay'.
- Spring 2000: the program went from 'no equipment' in January to 'five boats' by March.
- The team 'generally rows 4+ boats (4 rowers and 1 coxswain)' (FAQ). Rowing 101 states 'In the 4+s that CRLS uses, the coxswain sits in the bow of the boat. You will see the coxswain tucked into the bow hull. We call these boats bow loaders.'
- 'CRLS boats always have a coxswain on board' and 'CRLS usually doesn't race in sculling boats' (Rowing 101).
- Sept 23, 2015: 'Shipment of two new boats arrive from Pocock' — a photo-gallery heading on the Photos page. This is the ONLY named boat manufacturer tied to an actual CRLS purchase.
- Launches (coach boats), Spring 2016 'Launchapalooza': the team owns at least three launches, referred to by colour/name as the 'Solo launch', the 'White launch' and the 'Black launch', plus three gas cans and outboard motors. Safety supplies stored in the boatbay: life jackets, paddles, hand pump, boat hook.
- The team owns a grey CRLS boat trailer (referenced 2024) — athletes de-rig, load, unload and re-rig it at every regatta. A separate food trailer is stored at a City of Cambridge lot.
- 2012 CRLS crew grill was inaugurated (organized/assembled by Michael McKee).
- June 2012: at US Rowing Youth Nationals 'The guys are racing in a boat we're renting from the Bromfield-Acton/Boxborough team.'
- 2014 (archived) Boat Fund page: 'a new shell costs from $25K to $45K'; the fund sought $100,000. Sponsorship tiers: Gold $60,000 (sponsor a new varsity Eight), Silver $35,000 (sponsor a new varsity Four), Bronze $8,000 per rower seat. Sponsor name/insignia 'hand-painted in a highly visible location on both sides of a sponsored boat', repainted every 3-4 years over a 10-year prime racing life.
- 2012 (archived) Boat Fund: a three-year capital campaign to raise a minimum of $40,000 for new boats and equipment, kicked off by auctioning 'Moving Along', a 14ft x 7ft mural oil painting by artist James Richmond, suggested minimum bid $14,000, bidding closed Nov 30 2012, winner announced Dec 15; contact Owen Dempsey.
- 2023: 'the district is not allowed to purchase used equipment, so the parent board – and the parents – end up footing the bill.'
- 2025: a 'Boathouse Cleanout and Oar Painting Party' was held; gas for the launches is an ongoing volunteer need.
- Oar colours seen at HOCR: coaches raced 'with blue and white oars' (their own clubs); CRLS crews raced 'with black and silver oars' (HOCR 2023 post).
- Equipment vendors linked from the site's Helpful Links page (reference links, not necessarily suppliers): Vespoli, Pocock, Dirigo, Concept2, Nielsen-Kellerman, Sew Sporty.
- Cox boxes and speaker systems described in Rowing 101; an 8+ is 'about 60 feet long and weighs about 250lbs'.

## 5. Traditions, rivalries, mascot, colours

**Tagline.** 'At Home on the Charles' — used as the site's title/strapline and echoed in posts ('here at our home on the Charles River', 'CRLS Crew's home at the Cambridge Boat Club').

**Mascot.** Falcons. Confirmed by: the Falcon Award; the 'Falcon Unisuit'; team tents 'with the falcon on the sides'; sign-offs 'Go Falcons!', 'Let's go Falcons!', 'Row hard, Falcons!', 'Fly Falcons fly!', 'come and watch us fly!'; Instagram 'Our Falcons are back…and ready to fly!'; historic Twitter handle @FalconRowing.

**Team colours.** UNKNOWN as an explicit statement. Data points only: 1989-1999 the club rowed 'under the name and colors of Community Rowing' and Spring 2000 was the first season 'under its own name and colors' — but the colours are never named. At HOCR 2023 CRLS crews rowed 'with black and silver oars'. Novice uniform is a team-owned tank top with black spandex trousers supplied by the athlete.

**Motto.** UNKNOWN. The nearest thing to a program motto is the AAA selection creed — 'Ability, Attitude, and Attendance' — and the archived line 'if you find yourself behind, don't give up – PULL HARDER!'

### 5.1 Rivalries and challenge cups

#### Mayor's Cup — vs. Buckingham Browne & Nichols (BB&N)

The Cambridge championship. Site text: 'Dating back to the 1880s, this is perhaps the oldest high school crew competition in the United States.' Archived Championships page: 'Revival of a century old race. CRLS and BB&N used to race each other from the end of the 1800's through 1929 when Cambridge High and Latin stopped having a team.' Raced on the Powerhouse Course / Powerhouse Stretch of the Charles. Trophy presentation follows the racing. Archived year-by-year 1V results 2002-2012 recorded; CRLS boys took the Cup in 2011, 2014, 2015 and 2016; girls 1V4+ won the Cup in 2016. In 2026 the boys 1V defeated BB&N by 22 seconds, 'an enormous margin in high school varsity rowing'.

#### Latin Challenge Cup / Latin Cup — vs. Boston Latin School

'The two oldest public high schools in the United States.' The archived page states Boston Latin School was founded in 1638 and the Cambridge Latin School in 1641 — NOTE this conflicts with the current History page, which gives 1643 for Cambridge Latin School. Results recorded 2006, 2007, 2011, 2012 (girls). A 2015 post calls it 'an historic dual race culminating in one school winning the coveted Latin Cup', hosted on the Charles from around CBC to the Boston Latin docks.

#### Davison Cup — vs. Multi-team, Merrimack River, Hooksett, NH

CRLS 'kept' the Davison Cup in 2013 ('Several of the varsity boys were heard scheming to have it at home to eat cereal from') and won again in 2014. Trophy Case lists CRLS women's Davison Cup wins 2011-2015.

#### Duel of the Latins — vs. Boston Latin School

Listed in the Trophy Case under WOMEN: 'Duel of the Latins 2007, 2011-2013'.

#### Entropy Cup — vs. Brookline and Boston Latin

A home novice/lower-boat race on the Powerhouse stretch (documented May 17, 2025). Coxswains race more than once 'to allow the coxswains a chance to polish their steering skills'.

#### Cake Race — vs. Row Boston / G-Row / B-Row

Recurring spring race on the Charles (2007, 2012, 2014, 2015).

#### Historic 1889-1929 rivals — vs. Rindge Technical School and Brown & Nichols

The original Cambridge Latin High Crew raced these two in fours.

### 5.2 Team traditions and annual events

- Halloween Regatta — intra-squad racing in 4+s on the Charles with athlete-chosen lineups, an erging competition and a costume contest; explicitly athlete-only, no parents. The 2014 edition was the '2nd Annual'. Still running as of 2024.
- Pancake Breakfast — annual fall fundraiser at the Cambridge Boat Club; the '2nd Annual' was Nov 11, 2012; described in 2024 as 'the official end of our fall season'.
- End-of-season Potluck / End-of-Season Alumni Potluck at Cambridge Boat Club; also an end-of-season BBQ (2012 saw the inauguration of the CRLS crew grill).
- End of Year Celebration at the Cambridge Boat Club (spring, 2026).
- Pasta dinners hosted in athletes' homes before regattas, coordinated by the team captains.
- The regatta Food Tent — tents 'with the falcon on the sides'; 'NO ONE WILL EVER BE TURNED AWAY FROM OUR FOOD TENT'.
- Coxswain's Choice: on the last practice of the fall season, coxswains 'get to plan your own practice(!)' (2013).
- 'Launchapalooza' — the spring day when volunteers carry the launches to the water, install motors and inventory safety gear.
- Boathouse Cleanout and Oar Painting Party (2025).
- Erg-a-thon fundraisers (2015 team goal $15,000; 2016 held on the plaza at the Charles Hotel in Harvard Square, offering passersby a learn-to-row for a donation).
- FOCA Walk-a-thon around Fresh Pond and the FOCA City Run/Walk.
- Cowbells — 'We are so excited to cowbell our athletes this Sunday' (2025).

## 6. Daily life on the team

**Two practice shifts.** 'We have two practice shifts to accommodate dock launch times and shared equipment/staff at the Cambridge Boat Club (CBC), 3:30-5:30 and 5:30-7:30 PM.' Some land practices are scheduled at Russell Field 3:45-5:30 using rowing machines and lifting weights.

**Why the shifts exist (verbatim).** *"'Because CRLS Crew shares equipment, coaching staff, and boathouse facilities, and operates within limited dock and river space, practices are divided into multiple sessions throughout the week. This schedule allows us to provide a safe, effective, and high-quality experience for all athletes while ensuring equitable access to equipment, coaching, and time on the water.'"*

**Frequency.** 'Practices are 5-6 days a week. Only high winds, lightning and thunder keep us off the water. On those days, we do workouts on land.' Novices practise 5 days a week after school.

**What a practice is like (verbatim FAQ).** 'Most people walk or ride their bike to the boathouse. Generally, you meet with your coach, go over the practice plan for the day, get your boat out of the building, put it into the water, and go rowing. When done, you clean the boat, put it away and go home. However, it's likely that practice will also include running, an exercise circuit, stretching, running Harvard's stadium, erging, etc.'

**Transportation.** 'The fastest way to get to practice is by bike or walking. Buses/trains can run late.' 'The school DOES NOT provide transport to practices, only to competitions.' Athletes must ride the team bus to and from races and stay until the trailer is unloaded and equipment put away.

**Learn to row.** 'Do you have to do the Learn to Row to join the team in the Spring? No. We also teach new people to row in the spring season. It is strongly recommended that you begin with the sport as soon as you are interested.'

**Swim test.** Required, 'good for 4 years'. 'Swim 100 yards in a competent manner and remain afloat for 15 minutes unassisted.' Administered by the team at the start of every season, usually at War Memorial Pool. Fulfils the CRLS swimming graduation requirement. FAQ: 'Varsity/Novice rowers are required to know how to swim.' 'As a novice rower, wearing any lifejacket will limit your range of movement.'

**Novice pre-season, Fall 2026**

- Mon Aug 24, 1-3pm @ Cambridge Boat Club – Orientation
- Tue Aug 25, 1-3pm @ Cambridge Boat Club – Rowing in Barge / Handling Equipment
- Wed Aug 26, 3:30-4:30 @ War Memorial – Pool Swim Test
- Thu Aug 27, 1-3pm @ Cambridge Boat Club – Rowing in Eights
- Fri Aug 28, 1-3 @ Cambridge Boat Club – Rowing in Eights

**Novice definition.** 'Novice rowers are new to the sport – they are often freshmen, but could be from any class at CRLS.'

**Boat selection.** 'Racing boats are set in both seasons by Ability, Attitude, and Attendance. We put the best in the 1st boat, the next best in the 2nd boat, and so on.' Archived 'Selection to the Varsity' page calls this 'the AAA merit system – Ability, Attendance, Attitude' and explains testing (erg tests from 20 seconds to 30 minutes, timed runs, weight lifting, flexibility, core strength) and seat racing to pick the initial top 20.

**Boys and girls.** 'Do boys and girls row together? No, but while we do have separate boys and girls squads, we're all part of CRLS Crew.'

**Fall vs. Spring seasons (verbatim FAQ)**

| Fall | Spring |
|---|---|
| Season starts warm, finishes cold | Season starts cold, finishes warm |
| Focus is on technique, team building, overall fitness | Focus is on racing 1000m-2000m |
| Varsity and JV/Novice teams are often mixed | Varsity and JV/Novice teams are separated |
| Races are ~20 minutes long (3000-5000m) | Races are ~6 minutes long |
| Races are time trials | Races are side by side |
| Compete in the Head of the Charles Regatta | Compete in NEIRA |
| Compete in the Mass. Public School Championships | Compete in the Mass. Public School Spring Championships |

Also the same in both seasons: *"We generally row 4+ boats (4 rowers and 1 coxswain). We have Varsity and JV/Novice (includes new rowers). Races can take a couple of hours or all day and are usually on the weekends."*

**Winter training.** 'Athletes are encouraged to participate in another sport during the winter but may instead join the crew conditioning program. Staying fit is a year round activity! CRLS crew coaches run a winter conditioning program 5 days/week from Dec through the beginning of March using ergs, some weight training, cardio and FUN! A fee is charged but scholarships are available.' Historic indoor regattas: Ergzilla (Acton-Boxborough) and the C.R.A.S.H.-B. Sprints (BU Agganis Arena).

**Eligibility requirements before the season begins** — *"If ANY of these are not completed, the athlete will be sent home immediately and not allowed to return until everything is in order."*

- Academic standing: full course load of four classes; pass at least three of four 10-credit letter-graded courses with GPA 70+ on the most recent report card; passing grades in English, Physical Education or Dance if enrolled
- Parent/guardian registration with CPSD Athletics (select 'Crew (Girls)' or 'Crew (Boys)' regardless of Varsity or Novice)
- Proof of Sports Physical Exam (good for 13 months)
- NFHS Concussion Education Course certificates from athlete AND parent/guardian (good for 1 academic year)
- NFHS Implicit Bias Course certificate from the athlete (good for 1 academic year)
- ImPACT baseline concussion test (good for 2 years)
- Swim test (good for 4 years)
- Cambridge Boat Club waiver signed by all participating athletes
- US Rowing waiver on file

**Cuts.** 'Depending upon numbers, cuts may have to be made; will be based on getting all registration paperwork completed on time, fitness levels, attitude, coachability, commitment to attending practice and races.' (Fall 2026 cuts announced around September 11.)

**Uniforms.** 'THE SCHOOL WILL PROVIDE UNIFORMS FOR RACING.' The school provides a rowing unisuit that must be returned at the end of the season. Historically the varsity unisuit was purchased by families via JL Racing (a 'Falcon Unisuit'); novices wore a team-owned tank top with athlete-supplied black spandex trousers. Clothing guidance: layers, no cotton, close-fitting, socks always worn in the boat, athletic shoes for running.

**River geography the program teaches families**

- 'Downstream = towards the mouth of the river; upstream = away from the mouth of the river. For our purposes, downstream means towards the Museum of Science, while upstream is in the direction of CRI and Watertown.'
- 'The basin is the section of the Charles River that widens downstream of the BU Bridge.'
- 'The Powerhouse Stretch is the straight section of the river from Riverside Boat Club (on Memorial Drive opposite Trader Joe's) to the Weeks Footbridge (between Harvard and HBS).' A separate page describes it as 'A 1000-meter straightaway starting just downstream of the Weeks footbridge and ending just below the Riverside dock and boathouse.'
- HOCR is 'officially 4,702 meters'; the 3-mile race starts below the BU Bridge at the BU Boathouse and finishes upstream of the Eliot Bridge/CBC.

#### 2012-03-26 — Registration Forms

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Past Season Archive · <https://crlsrowing.org/registration-forms/>*

All parents MUST attend the informational meeting for Spring Sports on Tuesday, March 27th at 7:00 PM in the War Memorial Main Gym.

However, if you attended the Parent Orientation meeting this weekend, we covered the required material in the CRLS Athletic Handbook. In that case, you can simply

- Download and read the CRLS Athletic Handbook [http://crls.cpsd.us/media/theme/Pro-Cambridge/network/8009/media/CRLS/documents/athletics/CRLS_Athletic_Handbook_2011-2012.Update%208-28-11.pdf?rev=1], 

- Print and sign the final page, 

- Submit it, together with the forms we handed out, to Maryann C. Cappello, Athletic Director, CRLS, 459 Broadway, Cambridge MA 02138.

#### 2012-04-21 — Spring Training Thanks

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Past Season Archive · <https://crlsrowing.org/spring-training-thanks/>*

Our spring training week was very successful. A huge thank you to the parents who brought and served food.

On the boys’ side: Michael Bierer, Janet McKee, Claire Silvers, Elizabeth Bierer, and Sarah Callahan each took charge of a day and had help from Jennifer Hogue, Elaine McCarthy, Sybylla Smith, Georgia McKee, Roberta Goto, Narguès Guilhou, Liz Conlin, Marti O’Mara, and Chelly Salcedo.

On the girls’ side, Lynn Cadwallader and Mary White organized, with help from Richard Barran, Denise DiIanni, Nadine Berenguier, Win Lenihan, Chris Colbath Hess, Madhvi Patil, Dominique Stassart, Anna Swan, Janet Randall, Mary Ruggiero, Karan Marsh, Cyndi Mark, Jan Lerbinger, Judy Epstein-Fisher, and Chelly Salcedo.

(Thanks and apologies also to those whom I inadvertently omitted!)

Thank you to the coaches for spending their whole week with our kids when they could have been off on spring break!

Our next events are: races on the Charles Wednesday afternoon with Belmont Hill (Powerhouse stretch), G-Row (probably the Herter Park stretch), B-Row (not sure); and the GBL Regatta on Saturday the 28th in Malden.

Best,

Chantal

#### 2012-05-28 — End of Season + Upcoming Barbecue

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Past Season Archive · <https://crlsrowing.org/end-of-season-upcoming-barbecue/>*

It was a big weekend of racing. Thanks to all who came and helped, and thanks to Claire Silvers for organizing the great food yesterday and all season! Yesterday was the inauguration of the CRLS crew grill, thank you to Michael McKee for organizing and assembling it — judging by the crowd of rowers swarming around it, it was much appreciated. Thanks also to the parents who went out and bought extra grill food, watermelons, strawberries and popsicles to see us through the long day. A huge thank you to Tim Bass for organizing and driving our trailer, and organizing and setting up tents all season and these past three years. Thank you to all who have trained with him and are ready to take on this important job for next year. We are not always as lucky with the weather as we were this year!

A huge thank you to Coach Dale for driving the boats out to Worcester late Friday evening, driving home and out again early Saturday, driving the boats to Lowell Saturday evening, and repeating the process. Thanks to all the coaches who gave their time for this roughly 16 hour day yesterday.

On Saturday we had our boys first and second varsity and our girls second varsity crews out at the NEIRAs in Worcester. Coach Bill Becklean stopped by to see all the races — he gave all these boys their start, and now they are graduating. Bill was on his way to be the finish line judge at the Harvard Yale regatta. The NEIRAs are our toughest race, and we were proud to qualify. 

B1V4+ 5th in heat

B2V4+ 5th in heat

G2V4+ 3rd in heat

On Sunday we had roughly 40 rowers at the Mass. State Public Schools rowing championships up in Lowell. Our boys first and second varsity rowed together in an eight, and our boys 3rd varsity competed with other schools’ first varsity boats. 

The girls’ first varsity event was contested, delaying the awards ceremony for an hour and requiring the coaches to stay for another hour after the ceremony figuring it out. A boat in the travel lane had interfered with the race. A huge thank you to Mary White and Michael Arnott who teamed up — one riding the bus and the other transporting Coach Emily. Without this arrangement everyone would have been home an hour later!

B2x 3rd

B2- 1st

BN4+ 1st

B1V4+ 6th

B1V8 5th

GN4+ 1st

GN8 4th

G1V4+ 1st

G2V4+ 4th

Our next event is the end of season BBQ on 3 June, preceded by the Girls’ Latin Challenge race — see you there.

#### 2012-06-07 — US Rowing Youth Nationals

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Past Season Archive · <https://crlsrowing.org/us-rowing-youth-nationals/>*

Good luck to Andre and Isaac as they race the Men’s Youth 2- (pair) at the US Rowing Youth Nationals [http://www.usrowing.org/Events/YouthNationals.aspx] down in Oakridge, TN (click link for heat sheets & results). They and coach Matt left on Wednesday after school. Registration and practice time are on Thursday with racing starting on Friday. First up for our guys is their heat at 10:48 on Friday morning. Winner of the heat advances to the semi-final on Saturday afternoon. The rest of the boats in the heat must race again on Saturday morning in the Repechage (second chance) race. The top two from each Repechage will advance to the Semi-final. 

The guys are racing in a boat we’re renting from the Bromfield-Acton/Boxborough team. Thanks to B-AB for their help. 

Live streaming June 9-10, below:

Live stream videos at Ustream [http://www.ustream.tv/]

#### 2012-10-14 — How to watch the Head of the Charles

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/how-to-watch-the-head-of-the-charles/>*

by Coach Dale

If you have the time and inclination, use your bike and travel the entire course. Begin on the Mass. Ave. bridge. It’s quite a sight to see all of the crews warming up in the basin. Check the HOCR website [http://www.hocr.org] for times on Saturday when fours and eights will be getting ready to race.

Follow along the Cambridge shore. Stop at the BU boathouse and you can hear the starter. They don’t say much. They will call you up to the starting line with a simple ‘Cambridge…paddle’ it’s the responsibility of the coxswain to bring the crew up to full pressure before getting to the starting line without getting to close or to far behind the boat in front of them. When the bow of the boat gets to the starting line It’s usually something simple like ‘Cambridge…row’. Very low key. Quite the antithesis to the excitement the rowers are feeling and the prestige of the regatta.

After the BU bridge it’s a long swinging row around Magazine beach and the crews get very close to the shore as they come by the Magazine Beach Swimming Pool. You can often hear the coxswains and it’s a good place to take close up pictures. Then you can hear the first announcers at the Riverside Boat Club.

The crews move out into the middle of the river as they line up, and then go through, the center arches on the River St. and Western Ave. Bridges. This is a long straight stretch and a good place to take photos looking straight down on a crew. It’s a unique perspective. Look for parallel oars. You can also hear coxswains making their calls. Cross over the bridge you’re on and follow the race on the Boston side.

Next is the 90 degree turn to the left at the Weeks Footbridge. This is a tough turn and the site of a lot of collisions. Listen for crews swearing about getting tangled up with each other, and then, swearing when they remember the new rules and time penalties they’ll get for swearing! There are about 120 umpire stations along the 3 mile course looking for crews interfering with one another, missed bouys, etc.

On the Boston side of the river is the Reunion Village. Check to see if your college has reserved a space and stop back in for drink. On the Cambridge side are lots of vendors and fee stuff being passed out.

The Anderson/ JFK St. bridge has a 90 degree turn to the right. Most people who got tangled up at Weeks tend to play it safe here.

Follow along the river and you’re now entering the Dead Zone. It’s the beginning of the last mile and a wide and quiet stretch of the course. This is where a lot of rowers make the decision to call it quits. The excitement and crowd noise has worn off and it’s the first time they’ve had a chance to come to grips with how tired they are and how much they’re suffering. Winning crews go faster here because they focus on rowing well and look ahead to the finish line. You won’t actually see anyone stop rowing, but look for subtle changes such as slumped shoulders, looking around a lot, and not a lot of white water generated by the oar during the drive.

At the end of the Dead Zone is Dead Man’s Curve. Tired rowers, hoarse coxswains, a big, tight, turn and you must be at the Eliot Bridge. The US National Team Men’s 8 was headed for its 6th consecutive victory in 2007 until they got here. Overestimating their ability to turn, their starboard oars hit the inside of the bridge arch and they finished out of the medals. If you’re standing on the CBC and BB&N docks watch out – crews have ended up rowing right up onto them in an effort to avoid hitting other crews and the bridge.

Follow the last half mile to the finish on the Boston shore. You’ll pass through the Athlete Expo area. This is where most of the visiting crews are stationed. Lots of vendors, food and people.

Shortly after the expo area is the finish line. As simple as the start line is, the finish is even less dramatic. No announcers here. All the action is on the water and in the finish line officials trailer. Who won? How’d our team do? You can check results online at hocr.org or go to one of the results tents or boards to find out!

See you at the river!

#### 2012-10-14 — Head of the Charles

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/races-this-weekend/>*

[event post_id=”1048″]

#_EVENTNAME

#_EVENTEXCERPT

Full Details Here [#_EVENTURL]

Parent Volunteers

Schedule, map and contact information posted here [https://crlsrowing.org/hotc-parent-volunteers/].

Watching the Head of the Charles

- Coach Dale’s guide to watching the races can be found here [https://crlsrowing.org/how-to-watch-the-head-of-the-charles/].

- Crew Parent Susan Hunziker published an article offering advice for first-time attendees here [http://www.cctvcambridge.org/cctvcambridge.org/ifyouheadtothecharles].

- Video of a coxswain calling a race here [http://2012olympicvideos.com/video/rM0G-Bne8zQ/Head-of-the-Charles-2008-Coxswain-View-Pt-1-3.html]

- Video about the course here [http://www.youtube.com/watch?feature=player_embedded&v=5yEnO2kU6Hw]

- Boston Magazine article on the HotC, with lots of of statistics at the end here [http://blogs.bostonmagazine.com/boston_daily/2012/10/17/head-of-the-charles-regatta-guide-2012/]

Transportation

Walking, cycling, and the HOCR shuttle will be the best ways to get around this weekend. There will be NO parking AT ALL anywhere around the CBC nor near the FALS expo area.

HOCR shuttle schedule here [http://hocr.org/the-2012-regatta/spectators/parking-transportation/ ]

There is no parking at the CBC from 10 PM Wednesday until 6pm Sunday. Folks can still drive by and pick or drop off their kids and stuff for this weekend but once the regatta comes the traffic is a mess.

Alums Racing

#### 2012-10-22 — HotC Results + Upcoming

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/hotc-results-upcoming/>*

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2012/09/2f62ce8.jpg]We’ve just finished our 2012 Head of the Charles Regatta.

A huge thank you to our coaches Dale, Matt, Bill, Emily, and Gordon for sharing their time and expertise turning our kids into athletes and rowers. Thank you also for all the arranging and special coordination required by this event.

Results and photos here [https://crlsrowing.org/events/v-hotc-racing/].

A huge thank you to Lindsay Frazier for taking on general HOCR event coordination and to Jessica Boyatt and Lisa McManus who helped. More thanks are due all the families who hosted out-of-town rowers for the event, and to the parents and rowers who sold programs and set up fencing. Thank you as well to Mary Ruggiero for providing snacks, coffee, and juice in the back of the boat bay.

It was great to see some returning CRLS rowing alums around the boat bay. At least four of them raced for their colleges this weekend, and some did *very* well!

Our next event is Mass States next Sunday, then comes the Second Annual Pancake Breakfast on 11 November, and after that our end-of-season Potluck on 24 November.

#### 2012-11-14 — Winter Training

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/winter-training/>*

Winter is a great time to make boats go fast! Everyone did a great job this past fall getting into shape, now it’s time to broaden and deepen that fitness base and build strength. While it’s not required that you work out this winter to come out for spring crew, it’s STRONGLY recommended that you get involved in some from of exercise. Do a CRLS winter sport, something of your own, or one of the boat club programs – but get some friends together and DO SOMETHING! 

Winter Sports @ CRLS. 

CRLS Winter sports begin Monday, November 26th!

Basketball, Swimming, Hockey, Track, Gymnastics, Wrestling and Cheerleading are all available.

Go to the CRLS main athletics page for more information http://crls.cpsd.us/athletics/familyid

Other Options for Staying Active

For those not interested in these sports but who want to stay (or get) in shape for spring sports there are the following programs: 

- Work independently at the CRLS weight room 
The CRLS weight room will be open: M/T/W/TH- 2:45 TO 4PM.

There will be a coach or teacher on site to monitor for safety who you can talk to about how to use the equipment. Students can design their own workouts or work with their coach to get a program of workouts to do. Contact the CRLS athletic department for more information. 

- CBC Weekday Junior Winter Training 

CBC: Erg centered program with yoga, running, body circuit work run by coach Matt

St. Ann’s: Weight lifting centered program with erging, running, body circuit work run by Coach Dale

Dec. 3 – Feb. 16

5 days / week $300 3:30 – 5:30pm

3 days / week $180 3:30 – 5:30pm

No classes Dec. 24, 25, 31, Jan 1, 21

Register: download form [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2012/11/2012-2013-CBC-Jr-Winter-Training-Form.doc]; complete and mail with full payment as detailed in the instructions.

Contact: junior_rowing@cambridge-boat-club.org

Address for check: CBC Junior Winter Training, 2 Gerry’s Landing Rd., Cambridge MA 02138

- CBC Sunday Morning winter training 

Available to athletes aged 12 to 18

Erg centered program run by Boris Kusturic, the head coach at CBC and the Wayland-Weston varsity girls coach.

Dates: December 2, 9, 16, 30, January 6, 13, 20, 27, February 3, 17, 24, March 3, 10, 17

Time: 10am-12noon

Cost: $70 per person.

Limit: 20 athletes

Location: Cambridge Boat Club

Register: email coachingatcbc@gmail.com

- Community Rowing Youth Winter Training 

Erg centered program run by the youth coaches at CRI.

Dates: Nov. 26 – Feb 14

Time: Girls: 4:15 – 6:15pm/ Boys 6:15 – 8:15pm

Cost: 6 days/week $650.00 or 4 days/ week $450.00

Limit: ?

Location: CRI boathouse

#### 2013-03-01 — Welcome to Spring Season

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/welcome-to-spring-season/>*

Welcome to the Spring 2013 season! We’re very excited for the coming season – our 14th! Here’s a checklist of things that need to be done, and some optional items, before we get on the water on March 18th. 

Everyone looking to join Crew this Spring MUST do the following – 

1) Have a valid sports physical to start the season. However, if your physical expires during the season, you’ll have to stop practicing until you get a new one. The coaches have a list of the physicals that the CRLS Athletic Department has on file. If you have a question about your expiration date, contact crlsrowing@yahoo.com.

2) Complete an Online Concussion Course. See the “paperwork” link under Registration above. Again, coaches have a list of current Concussion Forms.

3) Be in good academic standing . See the team Registration page for details.

4) Complete the Family ID sign up See the “paperwork” link under Registration above. March 8th is the deadline to register for Spring Sports.

5) Complete the CRLS Crew online team registration [https://docs.google.com/spreadsheet/viewform?formkey=dDJtQWpPMFl2M3RWcXoxbmtWU0gwclE6MA#gid=0] .

6) Complete the CRLS Crew online race waivers. [https://crlsrowing.org/registration/waivers/] 

7) March 10th and 16th , Boatwork ! Both days at the boathouse from 11:00 – 1:00. We’ll begin getting the equipment ready.

Here are some optional, but good to do, things to get ready: 

1) February 25 – March 15th the CBC is holding workouts for anyone interested in getting ready to row this spring. Go HERE [https://crlsrowing.org/practice-schedulecbc-junior-workouts/] for more information and to download the registration paperwork at the bottom of the page. Everyone looking to workout must turn in THIS paperwork. 

2) March 6th from 6 – 8pm Cambridge Parks and Recreation Director Paul Ryder is bringing in a group of educators from the Positive Coaching Alliance. They will do three separate works shops, one for coaches, one for parents and one for Athletes. The three workshops are: The Double-Goal Coach®, whose first goal is winning, and whose second, more-important goal is teaching life lessons through sports; The Second-Goal Parent®, who concentrates on life lessons, while letting coaches and athletes focus on competing; The Triple-Impact Competitor®, who strives to impact sport on three levels by improving oneself, teammates and the game as a whole. This is open to anyone. 

First day practice Monday March 18: meet in the CRLS cafeteria.

First Week Practice – see the link above.

#### 2013-05-03 — Race this Sunday

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/race-this-sunday/>*

[event post_id=”1583″]

#D, #F #j: #_EVENTNAME

#_EVENTEXCERPT

Full Details Here [#_EVENTURL][/event]

A Note from Coach Dale

Hi folks,

I wanted to write in the hope of clearing up some of the confusion about why we wait so long before announcing who will be racing and what the schedule is. 

As of today, Friday May 3rd, I still do not have the schedule for Sunday’s races from the hosts. I can tell you that this morning they emailed me that the first race will start at 9am and the last one is scheduled for 3:40pm. As for what boats are racing when, and who from our team is racing – I don’t know yet. 

Why is this the case? 

As a primer, it’s important to know that most of the high school races we attend are run by the host team coaches. Primarily, most of these people have jobs and families in addition to their coaching and just don’t have a lot of spare time. Few, if any, of the athletic departments at the schools are involved in scheduling or running races. This puts the burden on the coaches – it’s the same for our team. 

To give some insight, when I am scheduling a race on the Charles, I take the following factors into consideration – 

1) weather – is there a time of the day that isn’t going to work

2) other river users – what’s happening on our body of water when we want to race. Often that means contacting all the groups that use that water and checking with them. 

3) What boats do we need for the upcoming race? The rules about who is eligible to row in each league are different and some kids are eligible row in one boat type in one league but not in the other. This requires moving kids around from boat to boat. 

For our NEIRA races we compete in 1st – 4th fours. 10 – 20 kids go to these races based on how deep the other teams are. Basically, any kid can sit in any seat regardless of experience, age, grade, etc. Kids can only row in one event.

For our MPSRA races we compete in 1st and 2nd varsity four and / or eight, 1st and 2nd novice four and/or eight, and sculling sometimes. 5 – 55 kids can compete depending on how many boats of each type the each team has. Kids can row in more than one event (sometimes). “Novice” kids are anyone who started rowing or coxing less than 12 months prior to the race date. Varsity kids can’t row in novice races but novices can row in varsity races. 

4) Attendance – which kids are we going to have for that particular race. Are the kids eligible based on the CRLS rules about attendance at school and at practice? Are the kids even going to be here or are they going away somewhere? Are they hurt? Sick? Otherwise not able to attend the race? 

I.e.; for this weekend – we found out yesterday that one of the girls we were planning to have race is going to miss practice today and tomorrow – she’s suddenly ineligible for Sunday. Additionally, I received an email this morning from a girl saying she will be out of town on Sunday. We now have two scheduled racers who can’t race and so the roster of who’s going to the race has changed. 

5) I only coach the 1st and 2nd fours at crls. Those are the only kids I know about first hand. We have 5 other coaches on our team who I sometimes don’t interact with for days. I give them as much information as I have about what’s needed for the upcoming race. They try to get boats ready to meet those needs and give me their information as soon as possible. If they’re finding out on Friday that a kid can’t be at the race on Sunday, they have to communicate that to me, along with any changes or needs, and I communicate with the other head coaches about what ‘my team’ has for boats, rowers, etc. and we work on a schedule. As far as I know, every other team is organized the same (and some have more coaches). You can see why it sometimes takes a while for information to be collected. 

5) What is the situation with the other teams that are coming to our race – if we suddenly have lineup changes, it’s a safe bet that so do the other teams. This can even result in teams needing to move things around or even cancel entire boats from the race (as we’ve done twice this season). Do they have kids that need to arrive late or leave early? What time limits are they under? Also, things like a team needing to share equipment between events – as we did this past weekend between our novice 8 boys and varsity boys and novice 8 girls and 2nd varsity girls – can affect the schedule. We accommodate them because at some point they’ll be asked to accommodate us. 

As a hosting coach – once I get all of this information collected, I can sit down and figure out a schedule of what, where, when and who – usually in that order. I communicate with the visiting coaches about general items in the week or two before the race but the closer I can get to the race date before sending out a schedule, the closer I can get to only having to make a few changes before race day. While this leads to information being sent out at the last minute. It results in less confusion since it’s more accurate and requires few, if any, updates or changes. 

I hope this helps to give you some insight into why we don’t send out schedule information or race rosters until very close to the race date. I know that can be problematic for families who want to try to schedule other things. I wish it were simpler – it’d be a lot less hassle and stress for me too! However, it’s the situation we have and we, (hopefully), give you the most accurate information in as timely a fashion as is possible. If anyone has any questions or suggestions about how to improve things, please feel free to contact me at any time. 

Thanks,

Dale

#### 2013-05-12 — CRLS Wins (and Keeps) the Davison Cup!

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-wins-and-keeps-the-davison-cup/>*

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2013/05/image561.jpg]

We kept the Davison Cup! Several of the varsity boys were heard scheming to have it at home to eat cereal from. It was a good day of racing, but I understand it was pretty rough at the start, with waves to the gunwales and coxswains being blinded by splashes. Under these conditions there were a few cases of racing interference. Our rowers and coxswains handled it with poise and maturity, putting to use the skills they learned from their coaches.

Thank you to coaches Dale, James, Anna, and Dan for spending their Saturday with our kids, and thank you to the athletes who were not rowing today but came along and helped nonstop.

Thank you to the race ops parents, Alex, Howard, and Winnie, and to Claire for food coordination, and to Mark and Helen for helping. I always leave out a couple of helpers – sorry!

Thanks to Winnie for the photo.

Chantal

#### 2013-08-26 — Preseason Schedule

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/preseason-schedule/>*

[table “1” not found /]

Registration

To participate in CRLS Athletics for the Fall of 2013 a student must do the following – 

- Mail a valid Sports Physical* and concussion course certificate [http://www.cdc.gov/concussion/HeadsUp/online_training.html] to the Athletic Office (CRLS Athletics 459 Broadway, Cambridge, MA 02138).

- Register [http://crls.cpsd.us/athletics/familyid] for the Fall season on the CRLS Athletics webpage

- Meet the CRLS academic guidelines: During the fall season, a student must take a full course load of four classes. Students who were at CRLS last year must have passed four 10-credit letter graded classes AND earned a GPA of 70 or higher in the fourth quarter.

- Register with the CRLS Crew. This Google Doc is our team roster on-line registration [https://docs.google.com/forms/d/1NWmZbYsI3fuOvn0NlunaOzq7-QaaqpEEOpu0_6feHc0/viewform]. We will send Fall information to the emails we have on this form.

* Sports Physicals – a Sports Physical has the words “Cleared for Sports” written on it, generally near the bottom. It is valid for 13 months from the day it was issued. If yours expires during the sports season, you will not be allowed to continue participating past that date until you get a new one. If yours is still good from the 2012-2013 school year, mail a copy of that to the Athletic Office.

#### 2014-02-19 — Spring 2014 Season

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/spring-2014/>*

Crew is a demanding sport and we are a highly ￼competitive program that requires student-athletes to maintain the highest levels of academic, physical, and civic excellence.

Spring Sports Sign-up Meeting 

Tuesday, February 25

2:40pm – 3:10pm

CRLS Main Gym

PARENTS – you may now begin the registration process with CPSD Athletics via FamilyID [http://crls.cpsd.us/athletics/familyid]

ATHLETES – online registration [https://crlsrowing.org/registration/] is now open!

The season officially begins on March 17th but be sure to check your email for information about pre-season workouts as soon as the ice melts on the Charles River.

IMPORTANT DATES 

April Vacation (4/18/14 – 4/27/14)

Our racing season takes no breaks. All varsity squads should plan on being in town and on the water during these non-school days. We will have a very rigorous practice schedule and one or more regattas.

#### 2014-03-24 — Fundraising for FOCA City Run

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/crew-team-fundraiser-for-city-run/>*

what : Fundraising for FOCA City Run [http://racewire.com/register.php?id=3691]

when : Sunday, 30 March from 1:00pm – 4:45pm

where : Meet at Christ Church Auditorium [https://www.google.com/maps/place/Christ+Church+Cambridge/@42.3755703,-71.1203721,17z/data=!3m1!4b1!4m2!3m1!1s0x89e377427187c9e1:0xb9a1c1bef3199a0a], Zero Garden St., Cambridge (near Harvard Square)

for : Rowers and some parent volunteers

wear : CRLS Crew windbreaker and other crew clothing

bring : Water bottle

parents : If you can help, please let us know at susanmfisher(at)gmail.com

Rowers will be divided into groups and will go door-to-door fundraising from 1:30pm – 3:30pm in different parts of Cambridge. At 4:00pm they’ll meet back at the auditorium to write thank-you’s and have a snack. The funds they are collecting will be for the FOCA City Run the weekend after, in which all CRLS athletes participate.

#### 2014-04-05 — Mayor’s Cup

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/mayors-cup/>*

On Saturday we raced BB&N in the annual Mayor’s Cup. 

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2014/04/MayorsCup.jpg]
The Boys Team accepts the Cup for another year 

Boys 3V:

CRLS 4:29

BB&N 4:31

Girls 2V:

BB&N 4:41

CRLS 4:57

Boys 2V:

CRLS 4:17

BB&N 4:24

Girls 1V:

BB&N 4:40

CRLS 4:49

Boys 1V:

CRLS 4:04

BB&N 4:12

The Boys 3V race was scratched after a college 8 interfered with the BB&N boat just as the CRLS and BB&N entries were on their final approach to the finish line in a very close tie. The BB&N boat appeared to many spectators to have strayed into oncoming traffic by hugging the left-hand shore, but race officials judged that the college boat was to blame and scratched the race.

#### 2014-05-26 — Mass. Public Schools Championships

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/mass-public-schools-championships/>*

CRLS did very well at the MPSRA championships. It was an eventful day, with weather delays and cancellations but no actual weather. The team picked up some gold and silver medals and everyone rowed really well. 

| B3V4+

| B2V4+

| B1V4+

| 2nd

| 2nd

| 1st

| Photos [http://schooler.smugmug.com/2014/Crew-Mass-States/]

Congratulations to our B1V4+ for qualifying for the National Schools’ Championship Regatta!

Many thanks to coaches Kate, Marcus, Bill, Shelley, and Kellen for all their work on this long day and every day for training our rowers.

Thanks also to Winnie and Ralph for Race Ops, and to Claire, Mark, Lisa, Roberta, Emily, Rick and Sue for food and grilling.

See you at the BBQ on 1 June!

#### 2014-07-01 — Team Uniforms

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/uniforms/>*

The official VARSITY ONLY racing unisuit will be adopted program-wide and can be purchased from our online store for a limited time.

Click below to place your order before the store closes at 11:59pm on July 25th !

| [https://store.jlracing.com/store.jsp?ID=16JBKC1FL9I5J21CM6J9FDAIFHJDD3CJJ63CH7F1CHCB29#]

| [https://store.jlracing.com/store.jsp?ID=16JBKC1FL9I5J21CM6J9FDAIFHJDD3CJJ63CH7F1CHCB29#]

| Boys Varsity : If you purchased a Falcon Unisuit last Spring, you do NOT need to purchase another racing unisuit.

| Girls Varsity : This is a NEW unisuit and all girls varsity athletes should purchase a racing unisuit.

The official NOVICE ONLY racing uniform is a team owned tank top that is lent to athletes at the beginning of every novice season. Black spandex trousers are the responsibility of the athlete. All racing uniforms (varsity and novice) are only worn during races and not during practice, guaranteeing multi-year durability.

OPTIONAL ITEMS 

All athletes are strongly encouraged to purchase three additional items to stay comfortable on and off the water. Both the windbreaker and long sleeve t-shirt will be available for loan to all novice athletes during their first season only. The white or grey polo will be worn to school on race days.

Click below to place your order before the store closes at 11:59pm on August 7th !

#### 2014-09-13 — Rumble on the River, Sunday 9/14

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/rumbleontheriver/>*

Tomorrow is our first regatta of the Fall 2014 season! We’ve entered FIVE boats in the Rumble on the River hosted by Community Rowing’s Harry Parker Boathouse. Visit their website [https://communityrowing.org/rumble-on-the-river] for more general information about the event.

Click here [https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/16ocPAZwRe9TURx60cPXjo9blA7IqWIxbnY7oeHeywXM/edit?usp=sharing] to view line-ups.

Click here [http://www.herenow.com/html/rowingRace.html?raceId=20111] to view race times and live updating results.

ATHLETES: 

Varsity Girls who are competing should arrive at CBC at 9:00am.

Varsity Boys who are competing should arrive at CBC at 10:00am.

You should wear your new racing unisuits, bring water bottles, wear hats & sunglasses, and use sunscreen. Be sure to also bring snacks and a change of clothes for after the race. We also recommend biking to CBC so you can bike back to CRI after racing for the festivities. All our boats will row back to CBC to be dismissed after racing.

SPECTATORS: 

Parents and athletes not racing should meet at the Beacon Street Bridge, just downstream from Community Rowing’s Boathouse (here’s a map [https://goo.gl/maps/MYIdg]). There you can see the crews navigate the narrowest part of the river, “the sandbar”, vie for a lane under the bridge, and make the tight turn into the sprint for the finish.

#### 2014-10-27 — Halloween Regatta, Friday 10/31

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/halloweenregatta/>*

On Friday 10/31, CRLS Crew will compete against one another in the 2nd Annual Halloween Regatta. Athletes will decide their own lineups for short races in 4+’s on the Charles River. There will also be an erging competition (details TBD). Finally, to end practice we’ll have a costume contest so come dressed in your school appropriate Halloween costumes to win prizes! Congratulations on a great Fall 2014 Season.

2nd Annual Halloween Regatta 

Friday, Oct 31st

3:05pm at Cambridge Boat Club 

Note: Parents, this is an athlete-only event! We know you love to support your children but we can’t accommodate everyone at CBC. We’ll see you on Sunday 11/2 at the Pancake Breakfast.

#### 2015-03-14 — First Week Schedule (UPDATES)

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/firstweekupdates/>*

Athletes who need to complete the required SWIM TEST will report to the CRLS War Memorial Pool on Monday , March 16th at 3:00pm . To pass the Swim Test, athletes must: swim 100 yards in a competent manner and remain afloat for at least 15 minutes unassisted . Athletes may not participate in CRLS Crew until they pass this Swim Test under the supervision of our lifeguards in our pool.

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/mayorscup2015/>*

On Friday 4/10, CRLS Crew races against Buckingham Browne & Nichols for the Cambridge Mayor’s Cup! Dating back to the 1880s, this is perhaps the oldest high school crew competition in the United States. We’ve entered only our TWO 1V boats in this historic race hosted here on the Charles River.

#### 2015-05-18 — Rowing in Summer 2015?

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/summer2015/>*

Updates on the CRLS Summer Sweeps and Strength Development Clinic … We’re very sorry to have to make this announcement, but for the best interest of everyone, we will be canceling the rest of the Summer Sweeps sessions. Unfortunately at this time we lack the infrastructure to offer a program that is of the caliber that you all deserve. We highly encourage you to explore other options like CBC’s Summer Sculling Program [http://www.cambridge-boat-club.org/index.php?option=com_content&view=article&id=421:summer-2015-junior-rowing-program&catid=119:junior-program&Itemid=236] and CRI’s Summer Skills Advanced Clinic [http://communityrowing.org/programs/signup/]. THANK YOU all for your understanding. We can’t wait to see you again on the water in the Fall!

#### 2015-07-23 — CRLS Crew Varsity Uniforms – Order NOW

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/fa15varsityuniforms/>*

The team uniform for returning athletes consists of three items: 1) RACING UNISUIT , 2) LONG SLEEVE LYCRA TOP , and 3) WINDBREAKER JACKET . We encourage returning athletes to buy these items so that we will have enough loaner clothing for our incoming Novice athletes. Please let us know if this creates a financial hardship. 

JL Racing : Racing Unisuit [https://www.jlracing.com/page.CambridgeRindgeAndLatinSchool.html]

There are other items in the store, which are totally optional. A few new items in the store are lite drywick tops for warm weather rowing, and bright yellow hats and visors. DEADLINE TO ORDER BY: 7/26/2015. 

Sew Sporty : Long Sleeve Lycra Top & Windbreaker Jacket [http://www.sewsporty.com/crlscrew/index.php?main_page=index&cPath=13]

For a limited time only, the jackets are priced at $57. This is $18 off the regular price. There are also many optional team spirit items, like duffle bags and hoodies. DEADLINE FOR DISCOUNTS and FREE BULK SHIPPING: 7/31/2015 

Email Cyndi Mark [mailto:cyndi.mark@gmail.com] with any questions.

#### 2015-09-23 — CRLS Crew Erg-a-Thon Fundraiser

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-crew-erg-a-thon-fundraiser/>*

First Fundraiser of the Year – Help Us Start the School Year Strong!

Saturday, Sept 26, 8:30-11:30AM

459 Broadway, Cambridge, MA 02138

Benefits Friends of Cambridge Athletics and CRLS Crew.

DONATE HERE! [https://www.firstgiving.com/Npo/20972/Donation?designId=20847]

Come to Cambridge on Saturday morning to cheer on the CRLS Crew team in the courtyard outside of the high school as they row relay style meters on land to raise funds for their team.

Teammates need to ask for sponsorship by friends, family, and neighbors this week. Athletes: the sponsorship form is here. [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2015/09/2015-Erg-a-thon.pdf]

The team’s goal is $15,000.

[http://www.friendsofcambridgeathletics.org/]

The crew team usually participates in the Friends of Cambridge Athletics (FOCA) Walkathon [http://www.friendsofcambridgeathletics.org/] around Fresh Pond, but this year it is scheduled for Sunday, October 18, which is the same day as the Head of the Charles Regatta [http://www.hocr.org/].

Our coaches created this erg-a-thon event so that the crew team can still support FOCA while not missing the HOCR. We are grateful for your support!

Photos from winter 2015 Ergzilla.

Parking 

Spectator Parking at the wharf area and along Adams Street in Milton will be very limited. We have arranged for automobile parking at the Extra Space Storage parking lot at 2 Adams Street, Milton (on the right after you pass by Wharf Street). We will have attendants to direct you to a spot. There is no required fee for parking.

Spectator Viewing 

Spectator viewing is best from either Pope John Paul II Park, which may be reached by the Neponset Trail (walking/biking) that starts near the MBTA trolley station (about 1.5 miles) or at the Neponset Park Finish Line (about half way along the same trail).

#### 2015-11-06 — Crew Winter Training Program

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/wintertraining1516/>*

To stay fit throughout the cold winter months and to get ready for the Spring 2016 season, the Cambridge Recreation Center is hosting the Crew Winter Training Program from Monday 11/30/15 – Friday 3/11/16.

Program Expectations:

- Must be a City of Cambridge resident between 9th-12th grade.

- Read the article about training for 2k erg tests linked here: http://goo.gl/fypHcv.

- Pay a non-refundable $300* program fee by check made payable to “Marcus Caimi” with the memo “Crew Winter Training” to Coach Caimi in Room #2204 at CRLS.

- Compete at two indoor regattas: Ergzilla (TBD) & C.R.A.S.H.-B.’s (2/28/16). Registration fees are not included in Winter Training payment. More information about how/when to register for these events will be made available soon.

- Maintain a minimum of 90% attendance (the two indoor regattas are mandatory).

* If this presents a financial hardship for any family, please contact the coaches to discuss other options. 

Click here to register for the Crew Winter Training Program by 11:59pm on 11/23/15 ! 

It is the expectation of both Coach Kate and Coach Caimi that all student-athletes wanting to participate in CRLS Varsity Crew in Spring 2016 will either:

- play a Winter Sport [http://crls.cpsd.us/athletics/teams/winter_sports] (CRLS, club, private, etc) OR

- join the Crew Winter Training Program

#### 2016-03-09 — Launchapalooza, Spring 2016

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/launchapaloozasp16/>*

The CRLS Crew team works best with significant volunteer help. The maintenance committee is asking for new parent volunteers of younger athletes to come to Launchapalooza on Friday 3/11 and Saturday 3/12 to get us ready for the upcoming Spring 2016 season. Please RSVP to Sue Fisher [mailto:susanmfisher@gmail.com?subject=Launchapalooza Spring 2016] if you’re able to attend.

Here are maps of each location: Cambridge Boat Club (CBC) [https://goo.gl/maps/6BMs6RneeyG2] and Newell Boathouse [https://goo.gl/maps/NtvdMHzHzRD2].

Friday 3/11 

- Time TBD at CBC, 2-3 volunteers needed to organize the outboard motors. Heavy lifting required.

Saturday 3/12 

- 10:00am at CBC, 1 volunteer needed to re-fill our three gas cans with gas. Will be reimbursed.

- 10:00am at CBC, 5-10 volunteers needed to carry our Solo launch to the water. Heavy lifting required.

- 10:00am at CBC, 1-2 volunteers needed to install the motor on our Solo launch and test that it works.

- 10:00am at CBC, 2-3 volunteers needed to locate and inventory various launch safety supplies from the boatbay including: life jackets, paddles, hand pump, boat hook, etc. and put them back on board all three launches.

- 10:00am at CBC, 1-2 volunteers needed to drive the Solo launch down the river to Newell Boathouse.

- 11:30am at Newell Boathouse, 5-10 volunteers needed to carry our White launch and Black launch to the water and tie them single-file to the Solo launch. Heavy lifting required.

- 11:30am at Newell Boathouse, 2-3 volunteers needed to pilot the White launch and Black launch back to CBC as they get tugged along behind the Solo launch.

- 12:00pm at CBC, 3-4 volunteers needed to install the motors on our White launch and Black launch and test that they work.

On Saturday 4/9, CRLS Crew races against Buckingham Browne & Nichols for the Cambridge Mayor’s Cup! #### 2017-06-18 — Lightweight Boys 4+ at National in Sarasota, Florida

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/lightweight-boys-4-at-national-in-sarasota-florida/>*

By Kate Smith

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2017/06/IMG_0513.jpg]This spring 130 Lightweight Boys 4+s, hailing from all 50 states, fought for a bid to compete at USRowing’s Youth National Championships in Sarasota, Florida. Only 20 crews across the nation earned their way to the National Championship and Cambridge Rindge and Latin School attended as the fastest Boys Lightweight 4+ representing the Northeast Region.

On Friday morning, June 9th, the CRLS 4+ came in 8th place in the time trial, qualifying them for the A/B semi-finals later in the afternoon. The weather conditions were miserably hot and humid, a stark contrast from the 48 degree and rainy weather the boys had left behind in Cambridge. With minimal rest between races, the CRLS boys ventured back onto the course for the A/B semi-final. There they faced who would be the ultimate winners of the boat division, Rye High School. After pulling themselves out of last place in the second half of the race, the CRLS boys finished 5th, narrowly missing 4th place, and qualifying themselves for Sunday’s B Final.

Saturday, June 10th, was a much needed day of rest so the boys could prepare their bodies for the biggest race of their rowing careers thus far, and in the case of stroke seat and senior, Chris Johnson-Harwitz, his final race. Major thunderstorms delayed a majority of the races on Saturday so the regatta officials were forced to alter the race schedule for Sunday. This change actually put the boys in a better position because their race was now scheduled for Sunday morning, which, as lightweights, is beneficial because the boys would not have to wait until the afternoon to weigh-in and then subsequently eat and hydrate.

On Sunday, June 11th, as the crews lined up at the start line for their final race, nerves were high. As the starting light flashed green the crews shot out of the stake boats with lightning speed. It was the fastest start the CRLS boys had executed all week and it helped put them into a leading position with the top crews for the first 500m of the race. Soon after, Pacific began to assert their dominance over the field and the fight for second place was on. CRLS was solidly in third and making impressive moves over the other crews at 750m into the race and at the 750m to go mark. Greenwich, the other crew representing New England in the B Final, started making a move in the last 500m that CRLS powerfully fought against in the last 250m sprint. Both New England crews started to walk down the crew in 2nd place, Gonzaga College Prep, stroke by stroke, nearly pulling the crew out of its second place position.

As the crews crossed the finish line it was a photo finish between Gonzaga and Greenwich placing 2nd and 3rd respectively, with times of 6:45.717 and 6:45.865. CRLS finished a close 4th place with a time of 6:46.849, 1.132 seconds behind 2nd, and less than 5 seconds behind the winning crew, Pacific, who finished with a time of 6:42.061. CRLS’s 4th place finish in the B Final declared them the 10th fastest Boys Lightweight 4+ in the country.

The boys couldn’t be happier and we couldn’t be prouder.

The crew is comprised of:

- Coxswain: Thomas McNulty ’18

- Stroke-seat : Chris Johnson-Harwitz ’17

- 3-seat: Caleb Mitchell ’18

- 2-seat: Harry MacArthur ’19

- Bow-seat: Simon Leek ’19

The crew is coached by:

- Head Coach, Kate Smith

- Assistant Coach, Emma Conover

- Assistant Coach, Emilio Gutierrez

The crew is supported by:

- Novice Coach, Mary Kay Kenney

- Novice Coach, Bill Becklean

- Athletic Director, Tom Arria

- The CRLS Parent Board

- Team family members, teammates, and friends

- Former Head Coach, Theo Pritz

#### 2022-05-24 — Girls 1V4+ win Gold at USRowing 2022 Northeast Youth Regional Championships!🥇

*Author: Mary Kay Kenney · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/girls-1v4-win-gold-at-usrowing-northeast-youth-regional-championships/>*

The Women’s Youth 4+ won the time trial against 17 boats, their semifinal, and the final, earning gold medals and the title of Northeast Champions, and a bid to the Youth National Championship in June in Sarasota, FL. They are the first-ever CRLS girls’ crew, and first CRLS Varsity 4+ to qualify for Nationals. The boat includes Isla Mitchell, Charlotte Moldrem, Grace van Bever, Anna Bellows, and coxswain Noe Shoor. 

The Cambridge Rindge & Latin Crew raced 10 different boats in the USRowing Northeast Youth Championship. In this regatta, only the top 6 boats from time trials, heats, or semifinals advance to the finals, and CRLS had three entries make it to the final round on Sunday: Women’s 2nd 4+, Women’s Under 17 4+, and Women’s Youth 4+. Congrats on some fine racing! 

If you would like to help support sending the crew to Florida in June, you can send a check here:

CRLS Crew
P.O. Box 400116
Cambridge, MA 02140

Please make checks payable to “FOCA/CRLS Crew”

THANK YOU!

A receipt will be mailed to your address. CRLS Crew is a non-profit organization under the umbrella of the Friends of Cambridge Athletics. All donations are tax deductible in accordance with federal 501 c3 regulations. Employer Identification Number (EIN): 04-3480847

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/05/B25992C3-36D2-47F4-A4B0-488CC4359A19_1_201_a-scaled.jpeg]

#### 2023-10-19 — CRLS at Head of the Charles

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-at-head-of-the-charles/>*

The Head of the Charles Regatta (the largest regatta in the world!) starts this Friday (10/20) here at our home on the Charles River. CRLS Crew has three coaches and five team boats racing over the course of the three day weekend. 

The races can be viewed through livestream hocr.org/the-regatta/livestreams [http://hocr.org/the-regatta/livestreams] (starting Friday at 7 AM), but there is nothing more fun than cheering for our CRLS Crews on the banks of the Charles River while they compete in this prestigious race in our own backyard. The greatest views are from the bridges (particularly Weeks, Anderson, and Eliot), and from CRLS Crew’s home at the Cambridge Boat Club (CBC). If you would like to watch at CBC, tell the attendants at the gate in the CBC parking lot the name of your student-athlete (even if they are not competing) and you will be let in.

*Be aware it will be hard/impossible to find parking near the river.*

Results can be found here. [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/results2/?job_id=8392&org_id=0]

The 3-mile race starts below the BU Bridge at the BU Boathouse and finishes upstream of the Eliot Bridge/CBC across from Christian Herter Park/Community Garden/Amphitheater.

The first races for our CRLS Crew family will start with three of our highly accomplished coaches:

Novice Boys Assistant Coach, Bill Becklean , Gold Medal Winner at the 1956 Olympics

#### 2023-10-30 — CRLS Crew – Mass Pubs Recap and Thank You

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-crew-mass-pubs-recap-and-thank-you/>*

Hello, CRLS Crew families!

Congratulations to the squad for a tremendous showing yesterday at Mass Publics! You can check the results here [https://herenow.com/results/#/races/21187/results], but TL; DR: the kids brought home a whole slew of hardware and a couple of trophies! Photos courtesy of John H. available in the Photos [https://crlsrowing.org/photos/] link above (password protected, email for password [MailTo:crlsrowingteam@gmail.com]). Well done, CRLS!

A quick note about donations. Thank you to all who have or are planning to donate funds for our regatta meals. If you need Venmo, please see the attached QR code. Be sure to note that the money is for CRLS Crew so that FOCA sends it our way. If you have given cash, please email me directly so that I can make sure I have the correct amount attached to the correct name for tax purposes. I am working with FOCA to get tax receipts into the hands of all donors. (This is important even if you don’t plan on actually using the receipt!)

Why are we always asking for money? Well, rowing is an expensive sport, and the school can only provide so much. (For example, the district is not allowed to purchase used equipment, so the parent board – and the parents- end up footing the bill.) With respect to food, as those families who have come out to watch our kids race can attest, regatta days are LONG days! Rowing takes a lot of energy, and that means a lot of fuel is needed, especially when the kids are on site all day and access to outside food sources is very limited. An important part of being a rower is providing your body with the right nutrients to produce the best results. Some kids can get the right foods on their own and some kids can’t. We work hard to make sure that ALL team members have access to healthy food on race days, which is why we need your help. Please know that we appreciate everything that our families contribute. We cannot do this without all of you!

Next up are the Halloween Regatta (10/31) and the Pancake Breakfast (11/4); emails about each will be going out today. The program also has a major anniversary coming up, so we’re working on plans for that. We’re also working on some gear and spirit wear options. Stay tuned!

Thanks again!

The Board

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2023/10/IMG_8759-scaled.jpg]

#### 2024-02-15 — Registration for Spring Season 2024

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/registration-for-spring-season-2024/>*

[https://secure1.cpsd.us/athletics/]

Registration for the crew season is now open! Registration opens Wednesday, Feb 14 and closes Monday, March 11th. If paperwork is not submitted before registration closes you will not be eligible to try out. 

1) Register with school here: https://secure1.cpsd.us/athletics/ [https://secure1.cpsd.us/athletics/]

Note: Sign up ASAP even if you don’t have all of your paperwork ready. Knowing who intends to try out helps coaches plan for the season.

2) You must have a current physical on file

3) You must take the following online classes once this school year: Implicit Bias, Concussion

4) You must complete the ImPact test this school year (a link will be sent by trainer if required)

5) If you are new to the team, you must complete a swim test to be scheduled at the school when the season starts.

More information on spring registration can be found on the CRLS Athletics site.

https://crls.cpsd.us/athletics [https://crls.cpsd.us/athletics]
