#### 2025-09-17 — CRLS Crew: 9/17 Update (Varsity Caregivers esp take note)

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/8426-2/>*

What a whirlwind September is! The number of curriculum nights, school council meetings, IEP reviews – I’m already exhausted and I’m sure you are too. Please read the following reminders. 

VARSITY CAREGIVERS: Some things have changed and we need your participation to make this season run smoothly. (Novice caregivers are hitting it out of the park in this capacity.) Please email us [mailto:crlsrowingteam@gmail.com] if you have questions, but please read our asks below! 

1) Sweatshirt pickup for athletes 

The final sweatshirt pickup for athletes will be this Friday at CBC from 4:45-6. If your athlete does not pick up their sweatshirt, we may reclaim their size to go to athletes who were unable to sign up by our order deadline. Please remind your athlete about this important team spirit item, and to see Ms Garcia on Friday! 

2) Sign up for a Subcommittee! 

We are hoping you will ALL sign up for a sub-committee (yes, this means you too Varsity Caregivers) . Think of a Sub-Committee as the pool of players to draw on that reflects your interests!

We would like everyone to do a little something, to model teamwork , to increase community engagement and of course, to disperse the workload for the largest team at CRLS. 

Sign up here: https://forms.gle/ut4E2g6fUh1nZMDU8 [https://forms.gle/ut4E2g6fUh1nZMDU8] 

Board members will contact you about participation through the season. 

3) Gas 

Gas is a huge burden for our coaches as it involves frequent filling and transporting. We have a couple of volunteers now. We had some kind offers to join the Maintenance and Boathouse Subcommittee to help with our GAS NEEDS but don’t have enough names. We really need you – please sign up above! 

4) Boathouse Cleanout and Oar Painting Party 

We are at capacity for the Boathouse Cleanout and Oar Painting Party this Saturday (see the website, crlsrowing.org [https://crlsrowing.org/] for our current calendar). If you’re on the list, Victor and Shannon will be in touch with details! Thanks to Mary Kay for helping to organize. 

5 ) Opportunities for Adult Rowing 

We had a lot of questions about learning to row as an adult! We highly suggest giving Community Rowing a try, which is an inclusive program with a world-class facility (I row in a Masters sweep boat 3X a week). They have one more Intro to Sculling (one person, 2 oars) program this fall, and you can try out Sweeps (one oar, 8 people) in the spring. 

If you have an interest in getting on the water, you can email Nonna Giunta at nonna@communityrowing.org [mailto:nonna@communityrowing.org] for more information or follow the link below directly: 

https://communityrowing.org/sweep-rowing/ [https://communityrowing.org/sweep-rowing/]

Thanks all – I’m dashing to another meeting at CRLS. Have a great night! 

Warmly, 

Izzy 

________________ 

Isabella Ehrlich, President

Association for the Support of CRLS Crew (ASCC) 

Visit Us: https://crlsrowing.org [https://crlsrowing.org/] 

Email: crlsrowingteam@gmail.com [mailto:crlsrowingteam@gmail.com]

#### 2025-09-24 — Uni Distribution on Friday 9/26 URGENT RESPONSE

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements, Team Gear · <https://crlsrowing.org/uni-distribution-on-friday-9-26-urgent-response/>*

This is an important post regarding team uniform distribution from Melissa Maldonado Garcia, who chairs gear and merchandise. Please read it carefully as we have some scrimmaging this weekend where the unisuits will be worn ( more information coming ). 

Uni Distribution 

Team unisuit distribution will happen this week! If your rower plans to borrow a team uni, please complete this UNI RESERVATION FORM [https://forms.gle/rMJo3B939K9Ea5br7]ASAP, and read the information below carefully.

NOTE: if you purchased a unisuit for your rower this season, that order is still currently in production. We anticipate that the purchased unis will be ready any week now or into early October (JL, the manufacturer, takes thousands of orders in the period leading up to Head of the Charles, so production times can be lengthy.) Rowers who are waiting for delivery of their purchased uni should borrow one from the team for use in the meantime. 

Questions: Melissa Maldonado at crlsrowingteam@gmail.com [mailto:crlsrowingteam@gmail.com]. 

Uni Distribution Days: All teams @ CBC 

THIS FRIDAY, September 26, 3:30-8pm. 

PLEASE REMEMBER TO TREAT ALL BORROWED UNISUITS KINDLY! Line dry only, NEVER PUT THEM IN THE DRYER! Also, CPSD policy requires the use of undergarments when wearing a borrowed suit! Rowers that damage or loss their team uni may be responsible for replacing it!

Thanks all for taking note of this – the calendar will be updated.

#### 2025-09-25 — CRLS Crew: Novice Scrimmage vs Arlington Belmont and BUA Saturday September 27

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements, Novice · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-crew-novice-scrimmage-vs-arlington-belmont-and-bua-saturday-september-27/>*

Hello Novice Athletes and Families! 

We have an unexpected invite to scrimmage with Arlington Belmont (AB) and Boston University Academy this Saturday, September 27 at Spy Pond in Arlington. We do not have to bring equipment, AB will be providing boats and oars. 

Please note: Novice rowers will NOT be rowing at the Textile Regatta next weekend. Coaches will do their best to put as many athletes in races but we have to make the numbers of seats work with our competition. If 16 kids show up on time, we can only use 14 for an eight and a four and there will be 2 observers. This is a great opportunity to see your athlete in action!

ATHLETE TRAVEL (MAP ATTACHED BELOW) 

Athletes need to arrive at Spy Pond by 8 am Saturday morning 9/27. Racing will commence at 9am. Spy Pond and the AB Boat Cage are right on the Minuteman Bikeway, a bit more than a mile from Alewife Station. Bike riders can ride there and athletes can also take the T to Alewife and catch the 350 bus which goes up Massachusetts Ave. They should get off at Franklin St (8 stops after Alewife.)

• The Directory for this season [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/] for carpooling is linked in the password protected portion of the website. 

ATHLETE LOGISTICS 

• Send your coach a note if you are coming just as a supporter, not a competitor (several have told Coaches they can only attend briefly and we want to make sure we have enough butts in seats with which to compete). Coaches will do their best to get everyone into a boat BUT they have to make the numbers work. 

• THIS IS A WET LAUNCH! There is no dock. Athletes have to wade into the water so they need to wear water shoes, crocs, sandals, etc. Then they will get into the boat by pairs and THEN put on their socks to row. 

• Bring water bottles. 

• Dress for the weather – it should be sunny and mild. Layers! 

SPECTATOR LOGISTICS 

Spectators are welcome and encouraged. On Google maps, it’s Spy Pond Boat launch next to Spy Pond Playground. There is a very nice park along the bike path which hugs the Pond shoreline so pretty good viewing of the action. NOTE: the AB “boathouse” or cage is away from the water along Pond lane. Head to the Spy Pond Boat launch! 

• Each heat will be 1 vs 1. The first 1k will be raced, then, after the boats spin and catch their breaths (3 minutes), a rematch in the opposite direction. 

• Map attached below. 

•The Directory for this season [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/] for carpooling is linked in the password protected portion of the website. 

Parking: There is some parking near the boat ramp. There is additional parking near the Arlington Boys and Girls Club. 

RACE OPS AND FOOD 

A small CRLS tent will be set up near the boat ramp OR the picnic tables. Pre-race fruit/fruit snacks and/or post race donuts will be served. 

Carroll and Amy (Race Ops Co-chairs) will be emailing the Regatta and Race Ops Committee about support. However, as this is last minute, we could use additional help. Please email: regattacrlscrew@gmail.com [mailto:regattacrlscrew@gmail.com] if you can assist and thanks in advance. 

We sincerely appreciate all Novice families supporting their athletes in this exciting (and local) opportunity! Special thanks to the Coaches for getting it organized and we’ll see you on Saturday! Please let us know if there are any questions or concerns. 

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/09/Screenshot-2025-09-24-at-2.43.34-PM.png]

#### 2025-10-02 — CRLS Crew: Textile River Regatta Information 10/5 (+ important Novice info)

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements, Boys Varsity, Girls Varsity, Regatta, Regatta Food · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-crew-textile-river-regatta-information-10-5-important-novice-info/>*

Our first Regatta is this weekend on Sunday in Lowell! The weather is forecast to be sunny and gorgeous, and we’re excited to reconnect with all of you to support our athletes.

1) Textile River Regatta – Sunday, October 5, 2025 

The varsity team and select novice are rowing at the Textile River Regatta. [https://www.textileriverregatta.org/2025/] Lineups will be confirmed at the end of the week and communicated by the coaches to the athletes. Select novice rowers who will be competing will be contacted directly by their coaches. 

>>Note: Novices are expected to race NEXT Sunday 10/12 (Worcester) and more info will come out next week.

All Regatta information (including parking, which is $20) can be found here [https://www.textileriverregatta.org/2025/]. Families interested in carpooling can coordinate by using our family directory! The family directory is located on the password-protected Caregivers + Team section of our website. [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/] If you do not have the password, please email us at crlsrowingteam@gmail.com [mailto:crlsrowingteam@gmail.com].

We will have food and changing tents set up at the course. We have requested space in Area A on the below map. Look for our tents with the falcon on the sides!

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/image.png]

2) Athlete Logistics ( note transportation waiver info below )

All rowers are required to ride the bus to and from the regatta. Estimated times are as follows:

6:00 AM – Athletes arrive at CBC

6:15 AM – Bus departs from CBC

REGATTA

4:30 PM – Bus Departs Race Course

5:15 PM – Arrive at CBC, all rowers unload trailer

6:15 PM – Dismissed

All rowers must remain at CBC until the trailer is unloaded and equipment is put away. 

US Rowing Waiver: All athletes must have a valid US Rowing waiver on file. Athletes, you have received a link to your CPSD email to sign up or renew. A separate email will be coming about this important paperwork.

Transportation Waiver: Per CRLS policy, all athletes must ride the bus to/from races. Any family that needs to drive their own child must submit an Athletic Transportation Release Form [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/Athletics_Transportation_Release_Form.pdf] to the CRLS Athletics Department at least 48 hours in advance (so, before 6:00am on Friday, 10/3) The form is linked and is also on the website under Caregivers and Team. Please send it to the coach [mailto:crls@cambridge-boat-club.org] and the athletic director [mailto:tarria@cpsd.us] . 

3) Event Schedule (as of 10/1, subject to change)

8:50 AM Men’s Junior 4+ B – Event 17

9:10 AM Women’s Junior 4+ B – Event 18

11:25 AM Men’s Junior 4+ A – Event 36

12:40 PM Women’s Junior 4+ A – Event 37

2:10 Men’s Jr 8+ B – Event 53

2:20 Women’s Jr 8+ B – Event 55

4) Food + Volunteers 

We are serving breakfast and lunch to the athletes, as well as snacks. All food will be nut-free however may not be nut-safe (processing facility). There will be vegetarian options available.

• Volunteers Needed! We are feeding 60+ athletes, plus caregivers and coaches. Please sign up to help volunteer here [https://signup.com/client/invitation2/secure/70198812042/false#/invitation] (https://signup.com/go/AJhhLtL [https://signup.com/go/AJhhLtL]) with food/Regatta operations. We need you!

• We are required by the Regatta to provide two Volunteers (shifts of 4 hours) to assist with running the race. The following shifts are available: 7-11 am, 11-2 pm and 2-4:30 pm. Please sign up here [https://www.textileriverregatta.org/2025/] if you can help. We’ll continue to bug you until we have volunteers – no rowing experience is necessary.

5) Pasta Dinners, Saturday October 4 

Captains will be coordinating pasta dinners, so encourage your athletes to reach out to them for info. Thanks to the families hosting this time around – we still need homes for upcoming dinners . Caregivers, please sign up [https://forms.gle/nZSndRoUvD5ogoGJ7] if you can provide your home or can consider co-hosting (supporting main host). Co-hosting is a great way to meet other athletes and caregivers on the team! 

Girls Varsity: hosted by Isla and Ada

Boys Varsity: hosted by Roland and co-host (TBD – please sign up! [https://forms.gle/nZSndRoUvD5ogoGJ7]).

*Dinners this weekend are Varsity only events 

Thank you so much for reading this far. We are so excited to cowbell our athletes this Sunday – let’s go Cambridge! Any questions – always reach out (after you read this please).

#### 2025-10-17 — CRLS Crew: Head of the Charles Regatta

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements, Regatta · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-crew-head-of-the-charles-regatta/>*

The Head of the Charles Regatta starts today (10/17) here at our home on the Charles River. We have coaches (Bill, Seth, Bailey, Aleks), alumni and athletes competing – events are listed here [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries?job_id=9637&org_id=0].

The races can be viewed through livestream hocr.org/the-regatta/livestreams [http://hocr.org/the-regatta/livestreams] (starting Friday). The 3-mile race starts below the BU Bridge at the BU Boathouse and finishes upstream of the Eliot Bridge/CBC across from Christian Herter Park/Community Garden/Amphitheater.

If you would like to watch at Cambridge Boat Club (CBC), tell the attendants at the gate in the CBC parking lot the name of your student-athlete (even if they are not competing) and you will be let in.

(It will be hard/impossible to find parking near the river).

Results can be found here. [https://hocr.org/the-regatta/results/]

Finally, there is a wonderful article about our very own Coach Bill, who epitomizes the spirit of rowing and will be coxing this weekend, here [https://news.harvard.edu/gazette/story/2025/10/alum-to-steer-octogenarian-crew-at-head-of-the-charles/] for your reading pleasure.

Let’s go Falcons!

#### 2025-10-22 — MPSRA Fall Championship Regatta Information Sunday, 10/26

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements, Boys Varsity, Girls Varsity, Novice, Regatta, Regatta Food · <https://crlsrowing.org/mpsra-fall-championship-regatta-information-sunday-10-26/>*

Our last Regatta – Mass Publics (MPSRA Fall Championship) is this weekend on Sunday in Lowell!

1) MPSRA Fall Championship – Sunday, October 26, 2025 

(Almost all of) The varsity and novice teams are rowing at the MPSRA [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/index.jsp?job_id=9463] (otherwise known as Mass Pubs). Lineups will be confirmed at the end of the week and communicated by the coaches to the athletes. 

All Regatta information (including parking, which is $20) can be found here [http://mpsra.org/?page_id=96]. Families interested in carpooling can coordinate by using our family directory! The family directory is located on the password-protected Caregivers + Team section of our website. [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/] Please reach out if you need the password.

2) Athlete Logistics 

Details are coming , anticipated departure from CBC for athletes is 7 am (6:45 arrival) on Sunday 10/26.

Transportation Waiver: Per CRLS policy, all athletes must ride the bus to/from races. Any family that needs to drive their own child must submit an Athletic Transportation Release Form [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/05/Athletics_Transportation_Release_Form.pdf] to the CRLS Athletics Department at least 48 hours in advance (so, before 7:00am on Friday, 10/25) The form is linked and is also on the website under Caregivers and Team. Please send it to the coach [mailto:crls@cambridge-boat-club.org] and the athletic director [mailto:tarria@cpsd.us] . 

3) Event Schedule (as of 10/22, subject to change)

10:15 am Boys 1st Novice 8+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=15], Boys 2nd Novice 8+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=32], Girls 1st Novice 8+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=16], Girls 2nd Novice 8+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=34], Boys 1st Novice 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=1], Girls 1st Novice 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=29], Boys 1st Varsity 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=23], Boys 2nd Varsity 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=4], Boys 3rd Varsity 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=22], Boys 4th Varsity 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=30]

11:30 am Girls 1st Varsity 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=33], Girls 2nd Varsity 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=11], Girls 3rd Varsity 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=26]

12:00 pm Girls 4th Varsity 4+ [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/entries/competitors?job_id=9463&event_id=19]

We realize this is a little vague – it’s all we’ve got! Thanks for your patience. 

4) Food + Volunteers 

We are serving breakfast and lunch to the athletes, as well as snacks. All food will be nut-free however may not be nut-safe (processing facility). There will be vegetarian options available.

• Volunteers Needed! We are feeding 90+ athletes, plus caregivers and coaches. Please sign up to help volunteer here [https://signup.com/client/invitation2/secure/49258150009/false#/invitation]) with food/Regatta operations. We need you!

• We are required by the Regatta to provide FOUR Volunteers (shifts of 4-ish hours) to assist with running the race. The following shifts are available: 7-11 am, 11:30 am on. Please sign up here [https://docs.google.com/forms/d/e/1FAIpQLSdwGJjmYhiZ70lkKsmuOxHuVe68zknJEhpNcwV3f8m6zrQzyw/viewform] if you can help. We’ll continue to bug you until we have volunteers – no rowing experience is necessary.

5) Pasta Dinners, Saturday October 25 

Captains will be coordinating pasta dinners, so encourage your athletes to reach out to them for info. Specific questions can also go to Board member Rodrigo Verdi [mailto:rverdi@mit.edu].

Thank you so much for reading this far. We are so excited to cowbell our athletes this Sunday – let’s go Cambridge! Any questions – always reach out (after you read this email please).

#### 2026-03-01 — Registration for Spring Crew closes March 9

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Season Registration, Spring Season · <https://crlsrowing.org/registration-for-fall-2025-is-now-open-2-2/>*

Registration for CRLS Crew for Spring 2026 [https://secure1.cpsd.us/athletics/] is now open via the Athletics Department. Please review the following dates and requirements for the fall season. The deadline to register for Spring Crew is March 9, 2026. 

Please note that the March 9 is a HARD DEADLINE. You cannot register your athlete past this date, so please make this a priority!

All interested participants must complete and/or comply with the following items BEFORE the season begins. If ANY of these are not completed, the athlete will be sent home immediately and not allowed to return until everything is in order.

1) Maintain excellent Academic Standing [http://crls.cpsd.us/athletics/academic_eligibility] 

- Take a full course load of four classes

- Pass at least three of four 10-credit letter graded courses and earned a GPA of 70 or higher on the most recent report card

- You must receive passing grades in English, Physical Education, or Dance, if enrolled

2) Parent/Guardian registers you with CPSD Athletics [https://secure1.cpsd.us/athletics/] 

- Select either “Crew (Girls)” or “Crew (Boys)” regardless of Varsity or Novice

- Parental permission, agreement to participate, agreement to the handbook

- Understanding of the Hazing, Bullying, and Chemical Health rules

3) Submit the following documentation to the Athletics Office via email addressed to cpsathletics@cpsd.us. 

- Proof of Sports Physical Exam ( good for 13 months )

- Proof of Covid vaccination

- Concussion Course [https://nfhslearn.com/courses/61037] certificates from the athlete AND a parent/guardian ( good for 1 academic year )

- Implicit Bias Course [https://nfhslearn.com/courses/implicit-bias] certificate from the athlete (good for 1 academic year)

4) Pass a Swim Test ( good for 4 years ) 

- Administered by the team at the start of every season usually at War Memorial Pool.

- Swim 100 yards in a competent manner and remain afloat for 15 minutes unassisted

- Fulfills the swimming graduation requirement

5) Take the ImPACT Test ( good for 2 years ) 

- Computerized baseline concussion test given to athletes at the beginning of the season; details provided each season for anyone needing it.

- Please note: This test is emailed to the STUDENT EMAIL following registration with CPSD Athletics

Questions? 

Please email any inquiries to anyone on our coaching staff to: crls@cambridge-boat-club.org [mailto:crls@cambridge-boat-club.org]

#### 2026-04-10 — Mayor’s Cup vs BB&N, Saturday April 11

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Boys Varsity, Girls Varsity, Race Schedule, Regatta · <https://crlsrowing.org/mayors-cup-vs-bbn-saturday-april-11/>*

Good afternoon CRLS Crew families –

MAYOR’S CUP 

The varsity racing season kicks off this Saturday, April 11 with the Mayor’s Cup . Racing vs. BBN will happen in the Powerhouse Stretch. If you’re new to the team, the Powerhouse is the straight section of the river between the Weeks footbridge and Magazine Beach. The course runs downstream (towards BU) and the best places to watch are from the River St. and Western Ave. bridges, or at Magazine Beach, between the river and the DCR pool. Racing is expected to start at 10:00am and will likely be over by 12:00pm – below is the anticipated schedule.

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/Unknown.png]

Results will be on Row2k.com [https://www.row2k.com/results/] after the races. If you want to know who won sooner, ask an athlete or coach.

BOYS NOVICE SCRIMMAGE 

There will be one boys novice 8+ scrimmaging against Belmont Hill at 12:15 in the river stretch along Herter Park. They will likely race more than one time but it should be over by 12:45 at the latest. Verify with your child if they are rowing.

Novice Boys arrive at CBC:11:15 

SNACKS! 

As the Mayor’s Cup is a small, local event, we will only have energy boosting snacks available immediately before and after the race (think fruit, Welch’s gummies and granola bars). Please make sure your rower has a good meal before they arrive at the boathouse.

#### 2026-04-22 — CRLS Crew- Saturday, 4/25  Race @Brooks

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-crew-saturday-4-25-race-brooks/>*

Hi Crew families-

I hope you are enjoying vacation week.

I want to start off congratulating the Novice Girls who rowed against Brooks today. They came in both first and second! They do not need to attend the race Saturday. If they choose to come and cheer on the team, they will need to come with their own transportation and cannot ride the bus.

Race this Saturday, April 25, Varsity VS Middlesex NMH, Brooks @ Brooks. All athletes should know if they are competing. This is listed as a varsity event but there will be some novice teammates. Thus, the reason this is being sent to all families. 

Buses will leave CBC at 11:00am. Athletes should be at CBC no later than 10:30am. As a reminder, all athletes travel to and from races on the bus with the team. Athletes are expected to unload the trailer and re-rig boats upon their return to CBC and won’t be dismissed until all equipment is put away.

We will be serving pre-made turkey and caprese (tomato, mozarella, basil) sandwiches that athletes can grab as they are departing from CBC on Saturday morning. They can eat on the bus or save it later, depending on their race schedule. On site we will have a snack tent with fresh fruit, chips, granola bars, etc. If this menu does not work for your athlete, they should plan to bring other food with them. 

If you plan on attending as a spectator please find the maps of the site, course, and schedule linked below.

The address is 1160 Great Pond Road, North Andover MA. It looks like it takes an hour-plus to get to Brooks. There should be guidance once you arrive as where to park. Parking is free.

If you cannot attend or for a better view of the race, you can watch livestreaming at Brooks: https://www.brooksschool.org/athletics/livestream

Results will be posted the next day on Row2k

Hope to see many of you this Saturday.

Thanks, Shannon

#### 2026-04-23 — CRLS Crew Boys regatta coaches message and results from Wed 4/22

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Boys Varsity, Novice, Results · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-crew-boys-regatta-coaches-message-and-results-from-wed-4-22/>*

Hi, CRLS Families,

The team hosted a great day of racing yesterday, focused on the athletes who have the fewest racing opportunities during the spring season, particularly the novices.

Teams from Brooks School in North Andover and Belmont Hill just upstream from us came for the afternoon regatta on the Charles. Both schools have very strong programs at the varsity level.

All of the CRLS boys crews performed very well, with the first novice 4+ winning decisively by 8 seconds and the 5V edging out Belmont Hill. In both races, Brooks was well behind. 

The second novice 4+ race was close from the first stroke to the last, which is the very best kind of racing, though Belmont Hill won by about one second. The third novice 4 rowed well all the way down the course, but Belmont Hill was faster in that event.

Prior to the CRLS-Brooks-Belmont Hill races, the boys 1V rowed a rescheduled race against BB&N for the Mayor’s Cup (the Cambridge championship). Our 1V defeated BB&N by 22 seconds, an enormous margin in high school varsity rowing.

Thank you as always for your support of your student athletes and your support of CRLS rowing. We have a team of committed, hard-working athletes from the development novices to the 1V, and it shows every day in practice and now in races, too.

We are seeing personal qualities like responsibility, attention, teamwork, and good sportsmanship throughout the squad.

Next up for the 1-5V is a race with Middlesex, Northfield Mt. Herman and Brooks in North Andover this Saturday afternoon. 

CRLS Boys Coaches

#### 2026-05-01 — CRLS Crew Saturday, 5/2 race @ Groton

*Author: Izzy Ehrlich · Categories: Regatta · <https://crlsrowing.org/crls-crew-saturday-5-2-race-groton/>*

There is a race this Saturday, May 2nd, Varsity vs Groton@ Groton. All athletes should know if they are competing. This is listed as a varsity event but there will be some novice teammates. Race map and schedule are below.

Weather Update: It looks like rain for most of the day. Send your child with a garbage bag for them to keep their clothes and gear dry. This may help keep ticks off as well. Remember to have them do a tick check when they get home tonight.

Buses will leave CBC at 11am. Athletes should be at CBC no later than 9:30am to load the trailer. As a reminder, all athletes travel to and from races on the bus with the team. Athletes are expected to unload the trailer and re-rig boats upon their return to CBC and won’t be dismissed until all equipment is put away.

We are looking into having catered sandwiches prepared for this weekend’s race. Athletes can grab them as they are departing from CBC on Saturday morning and eat them on the bus or save for later, depending on their race schedule. On site we will have a snack tent with fresh fruit, chips, granola bars, etc. If this menu does not work for your athlete, they should plan to bring other food with them. 

All athletes should bring a full water bottle. There will be a water bottle filler at the site.

There are lots of ticks at this location! We will lay down tarps for the kids to sit on and ask them to do tick-checks after the race but they will need to do them again. They can bring bug spray. Here are some suggestions on how to avoid ticks.

Volunteer opportunities:

1 person with a Costco membership who can shop for snack food. (We will provide a detailed list and promptly reimburse you via Venmo.)

1 person who can pick up the sandwiches from S&S in Inman Square on Saturday morning and deliver to CBC by 10:30am.

If you are interested in helping out with any of the above for the 5/2 race at Groton, please contact Carroll Magill (parent to girls varsity rower Imogen Wu) at carrollmagill@gmail.com or via text at 917-687-9107. Thanks!

If you plan on attending as a spectator;

Attached is an annotated map of the area.

The address is 282 Farmers Row, Groton, MA 01450 . According to GPS, this takes about an hour to drive there.

The boathouse and docks are located some distance from the parking area, so be prepared to walk. There is no parking at the boathouse. Below is info from Groton;

“access the Bingham Boathouse, which is right on the Groton campus, by driving around behind the hockey rinks, parking there and walking the ½ mile down the dirt road to the river. There is very limited parking at the boathouse, most of it will be needed for the visiting team’s shells. It is all right to drop off spectators at the boathouse.

The other option is to watch from near the finish line. Drive past the School on Rt. 111 (Farmers Row) and turn left on Rt. 225. Continue until you cross the river on a small bridge. On the bridge you will be able to see the boats coming at you for the last 250 meters of the race.” (you may be able to find parking on the nearby streets)

Feel free to email me if you need a ride to the race, I will send out a request to the families. Please include the neighborhood you are in. It may be helpful for those who might offer.

Results will be posted the next day on Row2k

Hope to see many of you this Saturday.

Thanks, Shannon

#### 2026-05-29 — Record Breaking Spring Season for CRLS!

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements · <https://crlsrowing.org/record-breaking-spring-season-for-crls/>*

This Cambridge Day article [https://www.cambridgeday.com/2026/05/28/crew-teams-shine-championships/] has you covered with wins from the New England Interscholastic Rowing Association Championship (NEIRA) and Massachusetts Public School Rowing Association State Championship (MPSRA) on May 23 and May 24.

End of Year Celebration at the Cambridge Boat Club

They will continue training until they leave on June 9th to compete in Sarasota, Florida at Youth Nationals [https://usrowing.org/national-championships/youth-national-championships] (the second girls’ boat and fourth boys’ boat to qualify for Nationals from CRLS and the first time ever sending two boats at once). #### 2026-07-12 — Registration for Fall Crew closes August 17

*Author: Marcela Millan · Categories: Announcements, Boys Varsity, Fall Season, Girls Varsity, Novice, Preseason, Season Registration · <https://crlsrowing.org/8777-2/>*

Registration for CRLS Crew for Fall 2026 [https://secure1.cpsd.us/athletics/] is now open via the Athletics Department. Please review the following dates and requirements for the fall season. The deadline to register for Fall Crew is August 17, 2026. 

Please note that the Agust 17 is a HARD DEADLINE and ALL steps must be completed before PreSeason which starts August 24th. You cannot register your athlete past this date, so please make this a priority!

All interested participants must complete and/or comply with the following items BEFORE the season begins. If ANY of these are not completed, the athlete will be sent home immediately and not allowed to return until everything is in order.

1) Maintain excellent Academic Standing [http://crls.cpsd.us/athletics/academic_eligibility] 

- Take a full course load of four classes

- Pass at least three of four 10-credit letter graded courses and earned a GPA of 70 or higher on the most recent report card

- You must receive passing grades in English, Physical Education, or Dance, if enrolled

2) Parent/Guardian registers you with CPSD Athletics [https://secure1.cpsd.us/athletics/] 

- Select either “Crew (Girls)” or “Crew (Boys)” regardless of Varsity or Novice

- Parental permission, agreement to participate, agreement to the handbook

- Understanding of the Hazing, Bullying, and Chemical Health rules

3) Submit the following documentation to the Athletics Office via this registration link [https://portal.cpsd.us/athletics/home.php]. 

- Proof of Sports Physical Exam ( good for 13 months )

- Concussion Education Course [https://www.nfhslearn.com/] certificates from the athlete AND a parent/guardian ( good for 1 academic year )

- Implicit Bias Course [https://www.nfhslearn.com/] certificate from the athlete (good for 1 academic year)

- Impact Testing, After registration, you will receive an email from a member of the CRLS Athletics Training Staff, that email includes directions and instructions for accessing the ImPact Test. It is important to read it carefully and execute the test appropriately. Once completed, the Athletic Training Staff will record this in your profile. ImPact Test baseline scores are valid for 2 years.

4) Pass a Swim Test ( good for 4 years ) 

- Administered by the team at the start of every season usually at War Memorial Pool.

- Swim 100 yards in a competent manner and remain afloat for 15 minutes unassisted

- Fulfills the swimming graduation requirement

5) Take the ImPACT Test ( good for 2 years ) 

- After registration, you will receive an email from a member of the CRLS Athletics Training Staff, that email includes directions and instructions for accessing the ImPact Test. It is important to read it carefully and execute the test appropriately. Once completed, the Athletic Training Staff will record this in your profile.

- Please note: This test is emailed to the STUDENT EMAIL following registration with CPSD Athletics.

5) Note from coaches 

- Depending upon numbers, cuts may have to be made; will be based on getting all registration paperwork completed on time, fitness levels, attitude, coachability, commitment to attending practice and races. Cuts will be announced around September 11th.

6) PreSeason schedule for NEW rowers only 

Monday Aug. 24 th 1-3pm @ Cambridge Boat Club – Orientation 

Tuesday Aug 25 th 1-3pm @ Cambridge Boat Club – Rowing in Barge/handling equipment 

Wednesday Aug 26 th 3:30-4:30 @ War Memorial – Pool Swim Test 

Thursday Aug 27 th 1-3pm @ Cambridge Boat Club – Rowing in eights 

Friday August 28 th 1-3 @ Cambridge Boat Club – Rowing in eights 

Questions? 

Please email any inquiries to anyone on our coaching staff to: crls@cambridge-boat-club.org [mailto:crls@cambridge-boat-club.org]

Get everything squared away now so you’re ready to row on Day 1!

See you at the CBC!

### About

*<https://crlsrowing.org/about/>*

CRLS Crew is a sport at the Cambridge Rindge & Latin School [http://crls.cpsd.us/] – the district public high school in Cambridge, MA.

We are a club sport in the Fall and a varsity sport in the Spring. We have both a boys and girls team and we compete in the Massachusetts Public School Rowing Association [http://mpsra.org/] (MPSRA) and the New England Interscholastic Rowing Association [http://www.neirarowing.org/] (NEIRA).

We are a no-fee , competitive public school rowing program that removes financial barriers and makes the sport of rowing accessible to all interested students. Our program is made possible through the support of Cambridge Rindge and Latin School, the City of Cambridge, the Cambridge Boat Club, and the generosity of private donors and community supporters. 

Today, we proudly serve approximately 90 student-athletes each season, providing opportunities for personal growth, leadership, teamwork, and athletic achievement. Many of our rowers go on to compete at the collegiate level, carrying with them the skills and confidence they developed through CRLS Crew. Continued community support ensures that future generations of Cambridge students can access these same transformative opportunities.

Our parent group, the Association for the the Support of Cambridge Crew (ASCC) is organized to provide support for the team at events and to help raise money for team expenses. The ASCC is organized under the non-profit Friends of Cambridge Athletics [http://www.friendsofcambridgeathletics.org/] (FOCA) organization.

### Where in the world is CRLS Crew?

*<https://crlsrowing.org/about/aftercrlscrew/>*

Each year, our student-athletes graduate and leave the halls of CRLS to embark on the next journey of their lives: post-secondary education. Click the map below to explore the destinations of many recent graduates.

powered by Advanced iFrame

### CRLS Crew Parent Board

*<https://crlsrowing.org/about/board/>*

We are the Association for the Support of Cambridge Crew (CRLS Crew Parent Board). We are organized to provide support for the team at events and to help raise money for team expenses.

Our board consists primarily of parents of active members of the crew team. We manage communication to families, meet monthly with the team coaches to help coordinate team needs such as fundraisers, regatta and race food and transportation, social events and management of the team’s uniforms. Working with the Friends of Cambridge Athletics (FOCA), we raise operating and equipment funds for the crew team. We appreciate the support of the CRLS Athletic Department and our generous community to help us continue to offer the chance to row to any student at CRLS.

Our Academic Year 2027 parent board members and contacts: 

- Shannon Finley , Communications & Maintenance (helping with boats and facilities): shannonfm@hotmail.com [mailto:shannonfm@hotmail.com]

- Carroll Magill, Secretary and Co-Chair, Regatta & Race Operations: carrollmagill@gmail.com [mailto:carrollmagill@gmail.com]

- Marcela Millan, Treasurer: treasurercrlscrew@gmail.com [mailto:treasurercrlscrew@gmail.com]

- Rodrigo Verdi, Regatta & Race Operations, Team Captains Liaison: rverdi@mit.edu [mailto:rverdi@mit.edu]

You can reach us individually or as a group for questions regarding volunteering, donations and events. If you have a question or communication regarding practices, your child or the team, please reach out directly to the coaches at crls@cambridge-boat-club.org [mailto:crls@cambridge-boat-club.org]. 

We need some subcommittee volunteers: 

- Photographer : We are looking for volunteers to take photos during the regattas. The kids row a distance off shore. There are a few bridges at our races that prove to be good vantage points. A good camera with a good lens usually gets the best results. We will set up a Crew cloud storage for the photos. They are nice to share with families and are great for the year book and website.

- Uni and merchandise distribution : A few parents expressed interest in doing this. Please reach out to me if you’d like to do this. If there are others interested, at the beginning and end of the seasons we need to distribute and recollect the uniforms. In the Fall, we open up a team store to sell merchandise. We can connect you with the person who did this in the past if you would like more info.

- Social media updates : Update the website that is on WordPress and post on our Instagram site.

Email crlsrowingteam@gmail.com [mailto:crlsrowingteam@gmail.com] for more information.

### Frequently Asked Questions

*<https://crlsrowing.org/about/faq/>*

About Rowing: 

- Where can I read more basic information about the sport of rowing? [#1]

- What about practice clothing and racing uniforms? [#11]

Learning to Row, Fall and Spring Season: 

- Do you have to do the Learn to Row to join the team in the Spring? [#2]

- What’s the difference between the fall and spring seasons? [#3]

- What’s the same between the fall and spring seasons? [#4]

A day in the life of a CRLS crew team member: 

- What’s a practice like? [#5]

- Do you row every day? [#6]

- Does everyone row all the time? [#7]

- Do boys and girls row together? [#8]

- Can we do other things in addition to crew? [#9]

- What if I can’t swim? [#10]

Where can I read more basic information about the sport of rowing? 

US Rowing has put together a library of sections that include a viewer’s guide, quick facts, a glossary of terms, and much more. Click here [http://www.usrowing.org/About/Rowing101.aspx] to access.

What about practice clothing and racing uniforms? 

Practices in the spring will be cold so expect to wear layers and limit the amount of cotton. Windbreakers are very helpful in the beginning of this season. Close fitting clothes are best so they don’t get caught in the equipment.

The students should acquire rowing shorts/running shorts for warmer practices and tights for cold practices. Socks must be worn in the boat. Please bring athletic shoes for running to every practice.

THE SCHOOL WILL PROVIDE UNIFORMS FOR RACING.

Return to Top [#]

Do you have to do the Learn to Row to join the team in the Spring? 

No. We also teach new people to row in the spring season. It is strongly recommended that you begin with the sport as soon as you are interested.

Return to Top [#]

What’s the difference between the fall and spring seasons? 

FALL

- Season starts warm, finishes cold

- Focus is on technique, team building, overall fitness

- Varsity and JV/Novice teams are often mixed

- Races are ~20 minutes long (3000-5000m)

- Races are time trials

- We compete in the Head of the Charles Regatta [http://www.hocr.org/]

- We compete in the Mass. Public School Championships

SPRING

- Season starts cold, finishes warm

- Focus is on racing 1000m-2000m

- Varsity and JV/Novice teams are separated

- Races are ~6 minutes long

- Races are side by side

- We compete in the New England Interscholastic Rowing Association [http://www.neirarowing.org/]

- We compete in the Mass. Public School Spring Championships [https://www.regattacentral.com/regatta/?job_id=10075]

Return to Top [#]

What’s the same between the fall and spring seasons? 

- Racing boats are set in both seasons by Ability, Attitude, and Attendance.

- We put the best in the 1st boat, the next best in the 2nd boat, and so on.

- We generally row 4+ boats (4 rowers and 1 coxswain).

- We have Varsity and JV/Novice (includes new rowers)

- Races can take a couple of hours or all day and are usually on the weekends

- We compete in the Massachusetts Public School Rowing Association [http://mpsra.org/]

Return to Top [#]

What’s a practice like? 

Most people walk or ride their bike to the boathouse. Generally, you meet with your coach, go over the practice plan for the day, get your boat out of the building, put it into the water, and go rowing. When done, you clean the boat, put it away and go home. However, it’s likely that practice will also include running, an exercise circuit, stretching, running Harvard’s stadium, erging, etc.

Return to Top [#]

Do you row every day? 

Practices are 5-6 days a week. Only high winds, lightning and thunder keep us off the water. On those days, we do workouts on land.

Return to Top [#]

Does everyone row all the time? 

It depends. If you’re on the Varsity, then yes, you row every day and race in every regatta. If you’re on the JV/Novice team, we try to put everyone on the water each day, but you may not row every day or race in every regatta.

Return to Top [#]

Do boys and girls row together? 

No, but while we do have separate boys and girls squads, we’re all part of CRLS Crew.

Return to Top [#]

Can we do other things in addition to crew? 

Sure. But do so with the understanding that if you miss practice, you’ll have missed some things and that will make it harder for a coach to put you into a boat when you come back.

Return to Top [#]

Can I row if I can’t swim? 

That’s a big concern and an unavoidable safety hazard. It would be best to have a conversation about your interest in rowing, and how to plan for you to take swimming lessons as soon as possible. As a novice rower, wearing any lifejacket will limit your range of movement. Varsity/Novice rowers are required to know how to swim.

Return to Top [#]

### Our History

*<https://crlsrowing.org/about/history-of-the-program/>*

Formed in 1643, the Cambridge Latin School was the second public high school in the not yet named United States. Located in Cambridge, Massachusetts, it was formed shortly after the Boston Latin School was established across the Charles River. For more on the history of CRLS, check out The Cambridge Rindge and Latin School: Yesterday and Today [http://www.cambridgehistory.org/publications/cambridge-rindge-latin], a book by John Langome.

The Cambridge Latin High Crew raced from 1889 until the stock market crash of 1929 ended the program. A small program, the team raced in fours against rivals Rindge Technical School and Brown & Nichols.

The program resurfaced in 1989 as the Cambridge Rindge & Latin Crew Club (Cambridge High & Latin and The Rindge Technical School merged in 1978) and began rowing with the Community Rowing program in Boston. While the club often fielded complete eights, the team rowed under the name and colors of Community Rowing. Driven to practice by teachers/coaches Linda Lipkin, Phyllis Bretholtz, and Tobe Korsgren the Club was enthusiastically supported by Athletic Director Bill Bates and it was their combined efforts that led to the inclusion of a CRLS boat bay in the remodeled Cambridge Boat Club in 1999.

Thus, Spring 2000 was the first official season of the CRLS crew under its own name and colors. The season began that January as a part-time, non-racing program with 12 students with little rowing experience and no equipment. By March, it had exploded into a six-day-a-week varsity program with 50 kids, five boats, five races, and two coaches. The rest, as they say, is history.

Today…

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2011/10/mastatesf06gv4-295x196.jpg]CRLS Crew is a sport at the Cambridge Rindge & Latin School [http://crls.cpsd.us/] – the district public high school in Cambridge, MA.

We are a club sport in the Fall and a varsity sport in the Spring. We have both a boys and girls team and we compete in two leagues, the Massachusetts Public School Rowing Association [http://mpsra.org/] (MPSRA) and the New England Interscholastic Rowing Association [http://www.neirarowing.org/] (NEIRA).

Our parent group, the Association for the the Support of Cambridge Crew (ASCC) is organized to provide support for the team at events and to help raise money for team expenses. The ASCC is organized under the non-profit Friends of Cambridge Athletics [http://www.friendsofcambridgeathletics.org/] (FOCA) organization.

### Transportation Release Form

It is strongly recommended that you NOT schedule activities right after a regatta is supposed to end. Team members are required to help derig boats and load the trailer and to return with the team to the boathouse after a regatta is finished to unload the boat trailer and put the equipment back into the boathouse. It is unfair to the other members of the team for individuals to not help with the unloading. ### CRLS@Home: The Powerhouse Stretch

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/powerhousestretch/>*

The Powerhouse Stretch

(Thanks to Brookline Rowing for this excellent description).

Some of our races run on the “Powerhouse” stretch of the Charles: A 1000-meter straightaway starting just downstream of the Weeks footbridge and ending just below the Riverside dock and boathouse. There is excellent race viewing, and you can pick your spot to see early, middle, or late stages of the races: along the Cambridge riverbank or from either of the Western Avenue and River Street bridges.

There is no designated parking for the course, and public parking can be scarce.

The map below is specific to the Cromwell Cup, but it’s substantially correct for other events: 

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/04/640ce0a8aa58d335a14d294d_Powerhouse.jpg]

### Signup Checklist

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/signup-checklist/>*

Instructions 

All interested participants must complete and/or comply with the following items BEFORE the season begins. If ANY of these are not completed, the athlete will be sent home immediately and not allowed to return until everything is in order.

1) Maintain excellent Academic Standing [http://crls.cpsd.us/athletics/academic_eligibility] 

- Take a full course load of four classes

- Pass at least three of four 10-credit letter graded courses and earned a GPA of 70 or higher on the most recent report card

- You must receive passing grades in English, Physical Education, or Dance, if enrolled

2) Parent/Guardian registers you with CPSD Athletics 

- Select either “Crew (Girls)” or “Crew (Boys)” regardless of Varsity or Novice

- Parental permission, agreement to participate, agreement to the handbook

- Understanding of the Hazing, Bullying, and Chemical Health rules

3) Submit the following documentation to the Athletics Office via email addressed to cpsathletics@cpsd.us. 

- Proof of Sports Physical Exam ( good for 13 months )

- Proof of Covid vaccination

- Concussion Course [https://nfhslearn.com/courses/61037] certificates from the athlete AND a parent/guardian ( good for 1 academic year )

- Implicit Bias Course [https://nfhslearn.com/courses/implicit-bias] certificate from the athlete (good for 1 academic year)

4) Pass a Swim Test ( good for 4 years ) 

- Administered by the team at the start of every season usually at War Memorial Pool.

- Swim 100 yards in a competent manner and remain afloat for 15 minutes unassisted

5) Take the ImPACT Test ( good for 2 years ) 

- Computerized baseline concussion test given to athletes at the beginning of the season; details provided each season for anyone needing it.

- Please note: This test is emailed to the STUDENT EMAIL following registration with CPSD Athletics

### CRLS Crew Athlete Meals

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/athlete-meals/>*

CRLS Crew Athlete Meals 

Why are families asked to cover the cost of athlete meals? 

The CPSD Athletics budget pays some of our operating expenses, such as coaches’ salaries, equipment, and most of the costs of transporting boats and athletes to regattas. Athlete meals are not included in the CPSD Athletics budget. 

The CRLS Crew Board coordinates a huge meal effort to ensure our athletes are fueled for their races and recovery. Crew families are asked to cover their athlete’s regatta meals or provide their child with food from home. The food fee goes to pay for supplies, the trailer, and supplemental food at all the regattas. The CRLS Crew Board works hard to keep these costs as low as possible and as such tries to keep costs between $12-25 per athlete. The cost typically depends on how many meals and snacks will need to be provided. An afternoon race (lunch and snacks) costs about $12-15 while a full day race costs about $20-25 (breakfast, snacks, lunch, and more snacks). All athletes and CRLS family spectators will have access to regatta meals regardless of their ability to cover food fees. 

While we make an attempt to provide allergy-safe food, we cannot guarantee that prepared food is allergy-safe. We buy as many pre-packaged items as possible and keep ingredient lists on hand for review. Please be advised that athletes with food allergies often bring their own food to races to ensure that what they consume is safe. 

Please visit our Nutrition page [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/nutrition/] for more information on nutrition for athletes.

<< Back to Regatta support [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/regattasupport/]

### The CRLS Crew Food Tent

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/food-tent/>*

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Unknown-2-scaled.jpeg] 

The CRLS Crew Food Tent 

It’s a big job to provide sufficient quantities of the right food to our rowers at regattas. Here is the food tent plan designed to fuel our rowers with coach-approved healthy food at regattas. 

Most sports nutritional experts agree that a few fundamental and simple dietary guidelines should be kept in mind on race day: 

- Eat familiar foods, nothing new or exotic 

- Eat moderate carbs that are low in fat and sugar 

- Drink plenty of water 

- The local regatta food tent is simple, healthy, and predictable: 

- Breakfast: 

- Bagels (untoasted) with cream cheese, sun butter, jelly, honey 

- Instant oatmeal (plain or with honey) 

- Granola bars (low sugar/fat variety) 

- Yogurt 

- Fresh fruit 

- Hot chocolate, water, coffee, and when possible tea 

- Main Courses: 

- Burgers & Veggie Burgers (Full Day Regatta Only) 

- Turkey and Cheese Sandwiches (Short Day Regatta Only) 

- Tomato Mozzarella Sandwiches (Vegetarian) 

- Hummus & veggies (Vegetarian) 

- Avocado mash & veggies (Vegetarian) 

- Sunbutter and jam Sandwiches 

- Sides: 

- Chips 

- Fruit 

- Yogurt Sticks 

- Cheese sticks 

- All Day Snacks: 

- Hanover Mini Pretzels 

- Skinny Pop 

- Chomp beef sticks 

- Welch’s fruit snacks 

- Premade, frozen sunflower, and jam sandwiches 

- Nutri-grain bars 

- Applesauce pouches 

- Fruit leather 

- Dried Mango 

- All-day beverages: 

- Water. 

- Gatorade 

- Hot chocolate, coffee, tea << Back to Regatta support [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/regattasupport/]

### CRLS Crew Nutrition

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/nutrition/>*

In December 2023 we had the privilege of hosting Christina Chu [https://www.christinaychu.com/], sports dietician and sports nutrition consultant at CBC to educate us on fueling our athletes.

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/Screenshot-2024-09-30-at-10.37.22 AM.png]

FUELING FOR ATHLETES: 

Her agenda included the following:

1. PERFORMANCEPLATES/MEAL IDEAS

2. PRE/DURING/POSTROWING NUTRITION

3. TRAVEL NUTRITION/RACEDAY SUGGESTIONS

4. NEXT STEPS, Q&A

The slide deck for the presentation can be accessed here [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/CRLS-Boys-Rowing-Team-Talk-Slide-Deck-1.pdf].

REGATTA NUTRITION: 

In the fall of 2024 we also asked for her advice on fueling for race days. The slide deck presentation for regattas can be accessed here. [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/MPSRA-Nutrition-Questions.pdf]

### Parent Handbook

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/parent-handbook/>*

This document is an introduction to the sport of rowing at CRLS. It contains contact information for the coaches, an introduction to the terminology of rowing, an explanation of the races the team participates in, what each of the seasons are like for the rowers at CRLS, frequently asked questions, safety information, and much more. It is a must-read for all parents who want to know more about this activity.

Parent Handbook 2022

ParentHandbook-spring-2022-1 [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/ParentHandbook-spring-2022-1.pdf]Download [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2022/03/ParentHandbook-spring-2022-1.pdf]

### Parent Volunteers

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/parents/>*

As a parent/guardian of a CRLS Rower, you are part of our official organization called the Association for the Support of CRLS Crew (ASCC for short). The ASCC consists of the parents along with coaches, parents of graduates, some Cambridge Boat Club members and others in the community who help to support CRLS Rowing. Parents, friends, relatives, supporters, and families are an integral part of the CRLS crew team. The team cannot race without the support of the parents and guardians.

Please visit this link [https://docs.google.com/forms/d/1cMxl2WVF7aqMHVX8D7B6qNsN-MoI6OAnBAtdh_kQKA8/viewform?c=0&w=1] to let us know how you could best help support our dedicated student-athletes.

### Regatta Support

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/regattasupport/>*

Regattas require assistance from all rower’s families …it is simply impossible for this team to exist without your help, and many hands make for light (and really fun) work!

We are the team for our children. While we sign up for specific roles, once on site we are one inclusive team. While each job has it’s specific responsibilities please know that in the end the job is not limited to just those responsibilities, meaning, if you see where help is needed, please help. If you are planning on being on site please sign up for a job as every parent is needed to play a role in this huge endeavor for our children. Signing up gives us the knowledge ahead of time to make sure we have appropriate coverage before landing on site​; if you can’t make it to the regatta, there are food prep tasks that you can do ahead of time to get us off on the right foot.​

Links to signup will be emailed to you. Please click on the below links to find out more information:

CRLS CREW: Athlete Meals [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/athlete-meals/]

CRLS CREW: The Food Tent [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/food-tent/]

CRLS Crew: Regatta Food Tent Volunteer Opportunities [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/regattavolunteers/]

### Regatta Food Tent Volunteer Positions

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/regattavolunteers/>*

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Unknown-2-scaled.jpeg]

Regatta Food Tent Volunteer Positions 

- Race Day Captain: The Race Day Captains provide general oversight of all Food Tent activities throughout their respective shifts. The Race Day Captain is responsible for delivering ice and the hot and cold water coolers, overseeing the set up of the Food Tent, restocking non-perishable items identified as needed, overseeing the breakdown and cleanup of Food Tent, and identifying Food Tent items that need to be restocked at the end of the day and providing that list to the Morning Food Tent Captain and/or Regatta Chair after each regatta. 

- 

- Morning: Arrive at the Food Tent by 5:30am on race day. Deliver the large filled hot and cold water containers. Oversee tent, table, and food set up for morning shift. The Morning Food Tent Captain should unload this at the Food Tent site. Receive food items delivered by volunteers. Fill and organize coolers. Set up tents, tables and garbage cans. 

- Afternoon: At end of the day, oversee food tent breakdown and cleanup, identify any non-perishable Food Tent items that need to be restocked (plates, water bottles, Gatorade, cups, condiments, etc) and provide that list to the next weeks’ Captain and/or Regatta Chair. Coordinate removal or disposal of leftover perishable food items. Ensure the food trailer is organized and closed up properly when clean-up is finished, assist Food Trailer Hauler in connecting the trailer to his or her vehicle, and remove/dispose of trash.

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/image0-rotated.jpeg] 

- Food Trailer Hauler: There are two Food Trailer Hauler volunteer slots per local regatta. The drivers use their personal vehicle to tow the food trailer to or from the race site. The tow vehicle must have a standard 2” hitch ball and a 4-pin light connector. Between regattas the food trailer is located at a City of Cambridge lot. The morning and afternoon Food Trailer Haulers have the following responsibilities: 

- 

- Morning Food Trailer Hauler: The afternoon or night before the regatta, hitch the food trailer to your vehicle, ensure that lights are connected, and park both in a safe location near your home. Drive the food trailer to the race venue, arriving no later than 6:00am on race day or alternatively arrange for afternoon drop-off the day before. Meet Race Day Captain, ensure you are at the proper location, position it in the proper location, and unhook the trailer from your vehicle. Assist Morning Food Tent Captain with set-up of tents and tables. If you have not signed up for another shift, you are free to leave. 

- Afternoon Food Trailer Hauler: Arrive at the food tent site no later than 3pm (or at the arranged time) on race day. Assist with food tent breakdown and clean-up as necessary. When clean-up is finished, hitch the food trailer to your vehicle, ensure that lights are connected, and drive the food trailer back to the City of Cambridge lot, making sure to leave it in the proper position with the wheel chocks in place. Make arrangements for key drop off at CBC, with a coach, or CRLS Crew Board.

- Advance Food Prep: Each task involves picking up, purchasing, and/or preparing healthy food selections for the rowers in advance of the regatta. *** Parents who volunteer and incur expenses may submit a request for expense reimbursement to the Treasurer 

- 

- Shopper: * Costco or BJS membership required.* Receive the regatta shopping list by Thursday and determine whether you will shop at Costco or BJS. If possible, wash all produce and store refrigerated and frozen items (meat, cheese, yogurt) in your home refrigerator and other items in the CRLS Crew coolers. Coordinate food transfer with the Race Captain. Options include: Deliver to CBC at 5 or 5:30 am, deliver to the race site by 6 or 6:30am, or make a special arrangement with the Race Captain.

- Coffee and Ice Runner – Pick up coffee and ice for the food tent. Deliver to tent no later than one hour after the athlete bus leaves CBC.

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/image2-rotated.jpeg] 

- Food Tent Worker: There are four Food Tent Worker volunteer opportunities per local regatta. Morning Food Tent Workers assist with Food Tent set-up (tents, tables) and afternoon Food Tent Workers assist with Food Tent breakdown of tents and tables and clean-up. Each Food Tent Worker has responsibility for the following: 

- 

- Beverages: Assists with setting-up the large hot and cold large water containers, replenishes the stock of beverages throughout the day, and breaks down and cleans up the beverage areas at the end of the day.

- Sandwiches/Burgers/Bagels: Maintains the bagel station in the early morning and the sandwich/burger station in the late morning. Volunteers will also set up the sandwiches in containers of ice for food safety and replenish the ice when necessary.

- Fruits, Vegetables, Salads: Cuts and serves prepared fruits and vegetables. Monitors prepared side dishes brought to the food tent.

- Snacks: Athletes are provided with nutritionist-approved snacks throughout the day. Snacks should be arranged on a snack-specific table and replenished throughout the day.

- Supplies: Ensures that there are sufficient dry good supplies (cups, plates, utensils) in the Food Tent area. Volunteers also set up/take down the recycling bin (white square recycling bin with clear plastic bags) and take out the recycling as necessary — putting the full bags next to the full trash bags during the regatta.<< Back to Regatta support [https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/regattasupport/] [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Unknown.jpeg] [https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/10/Unknown-1.jpeg]

​

### CRLS Crew: Rowing 101

*<https://crlsrowing.org/caregiversandteam/teamonly/rowing101-2/>*

Rowing 101 – Part 1 

For those of you who are new to the sport, here are some fun facts on what your student-athlete is doing!

- A rowing boat is also known as a shell. Boats may be rigged as sweep boats or sculling boats.

- In sweep boats, each rower uses a single oar. 

- In sculling boats, each rower uses two oars . Sculling oars are shorter and lighter than sweep oars.

- The sweep boats are the 8+, the 4+/4- and the 2-. When you are looking at a program or a website like RegattaCentral or HereNow (more on those below), you can recognize the sweep boats because they have a + or a – attached to their names.The sweep boats are also known as the Eight, the Coxed Four/Straight Four and the Pair. 

- The plus/minus tells you whether or not that boat has a coxswain! To make things easier, CRLS boats always have a coxswain on board. So don’t worry about the straight (-) boats (the Straight Four and the Pair) for now.

- The sculling boats are the 4x, the 2x and the 1x , a/k/a the Quad, the Double and the Single . (There’s technically also an Octuple, but those are rare!) You can recognize a sculling boat by the x attached to their names.

- CRLS usually doesn’t race in sculling boats, but there will be opportunities over the summer for the kids to learn how to scull.

- The name or category of the boat tells you how many kids are in it, not including the coxswain. So, the Eight has 8 rowers on board, not including the coxswain.

- The front of a racing shell is called the bow. The rear is the stern . The left side of the boat (from the vantage point of the coxswain) is the port side. The right side is the starboard side.

- The coxswain steers the boat by moving a cable attached to a rudder. The coxswain always faces forward.

- The rowers, whether in a sweep boat or a sculling boat, always face backward in relation to the direction of the boat.

- In an 8+, the coxswain sits in the stern section of the boat.

- In the 4+s that CRLS uses, the coxswain sits in the bow of the boat. You will see the coxswain tucked into the bow hull. We call these boats bow loaders. In days of yore, 4+s were constructed like 8+s, and the coxswains sat in the stern. You will likely see older 4 person boats at races that still have the coxswain in the stern.

- Rowers in sweep boats are either ports or starboards , meaning their single oar extends to either the port or starboard side of the boat. There is no exact science to how a rower becomes a port or a starboard; many rowers can switch sides.

- All sweep boat seats are referred to by their number, with the exception of the #8 seat and the #1 seat. The #8 seat, which is closest to the stern, is Stroke. The #1 seat, which is closest to the bow of the boat, is Bow. Every other seat is identified by its number counting down from the stern (Seven seat, Four seat, etc.) Rowers tend to move in pairs, so Stoke and 7 (or Stroke and 3) are Stern Pair. 2 Seat and Bow are Bow Pair.

- Stern Pair sets the pace for the rest of the boat.

- Bow Pair has the important job of keeping the boat balanced.

- In the 8+, Seats 6-3 are the Engine Room. Your Engine Room rower will assure you that the Engine Room does all the work and are the strongest rowers!

- Other fun things to know: the coxswain communicates with the rowers through an installed speaker system attached to a microphone and a “cox box.” The cox box is a small computer that provides the coxswain with feedback such as how fast the boat is moving, and how many strokes per minute the rowers are taking.

- The oars sit in oarlocks which are attached to the shell by riggers. When the team travels to regattas, they take the riggers off the boat (de-rig) so they can load the boat onto the trailer, which hauls the equipment to the course. The team then unloads the trailer, and re-rigs the boat to race. At the end of the racing day, the team once again de-rigs, loads, goes back to CBC, unloads and re-rigs! Racing days are long days.

- The 8+ is about 60 feet long and weighs about 250lbs.

- “Crew team” is redundant, like PIN number. It’s a rowing team or a crew!

- Downstream = towards the mouth of the river; upstream = away from the mouth of the river. For our purposes, downstream means towards the Museum of Science, while upstream is in the direction of CRI and Watertown.

- The basin is the section of the Charles River that widens downstream of the BU Bridge.

- The Powerhouse Stretch is the straight section of the river from Riverside Boat Club (on Memorial Drive opposite Trader Joe’s) to the Weeks Footbridge (between Harvard and HBS).

Next week we will discuss more boat parts and what it means to catch a !

Fall Regattas 101 

Here is a quick primer on what to expect at fall regattas. Fall is head racing season, and we are fortunate to share a boathouse with the most famous head race of them all: the Head of the Charles Regatta (HOCR)!

- Unlike a sprint race (those happen in the spring season, so check back in a few months for more information about those!), head races are essentially time trials, with each boat going across the start line alone, one at a time, in set intervals. Each boat is competing to travel the racecourse in the fastest time.

- Head races around the region vary in length (fun fact: HOCR is officially 4,702 meters), but are usually 2-3 miles long.

- Although boats are racing against the clock, there is usually some interaction between boats, especially when the course involves turns or obstacles like bridges. Head races almost always involve clashing of oars or collisions as coxswains jockey for positions.

- Coxswains have a very important role in head races as they steer and strategize to get the fastest, clearest course.

- The easiest way to tell how a boat is performing is to look at its bow number, compared to the bow numbers of the boats around it. All boats in a head race go off in numerical order, so if you see a boat with bow #11 go past before you see #9 and #10, that means that #11 has already caught up to and passed #9 and #10, so #11 is traveling down the course faster than the others.

- Nearly all regatta organizers use either RegattaCentral [https://www.regattacentral.com/] or HereNow [https://www.herenow.com/#/home] for their race management needs. These sites have all the info you need to know about a given regatta, including the race schedule, entries, heat sheets, parking and spectator information, and the results. Results [https://www.herenow.com/results/#/races/21042/results] are usually updated in real time, so you will know as your rower crosses the finish line how they did. Familiarize yourself with both sites now, and impress your rower with your knowledge of their event!

- Before each regatta, we will do our best to point out prime vantage points and helpful information for families.

### Make a Donation

*<https://crlsrowing.org/support/>*

Donate to CRLS Crew 

We invite to support our team by contributing to regatta and race food, and other season team expenses. 

For new and returning families we suggest a voluntary contribution of $75–$150 per athlete to help support our team throughout the season. Your contribution helps keep our athletes fueled on long competition days and covers essential parent board expenses that allow us to provide the resources and support our rowers need to thrive. Every contribution, at any level, makes a meaningful difference and helps keep CRLS Crew strong, accessible, and ready to compete.

We also invite our alumni, near and far, to support our team through tax-deductible donations via FOCA. Please note that we are unable to offer tax deductions for donations made through Venmo. Read the information below for more details on how to make a tax-deductible contribution.

We accept donations toward food and other team season expenses via Venmo at @CRLS-Rowing. You can use this Venmo link [https://account.venmo.com/u/CRLS-Rowing] or simply scan the QR code below to make your contribution. In the Venmo message, please include your child’s full name, team (for example, Girls Novice ), and the words “Fall Donation.” 

If you have any questions about your contribution, you can also reach out to Marcela Millan, Team Treasurer, at treasurercrlscrew@gmail.com [mailto:treasurercrlscrew@gmail.com]. 

[https://crlsrowing.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Crew-Venmo-QRCode-Flyer.jpg]

*Please note, ONLY donations made through The Friends of Cambridge Athletics (FOCA) are tax deductible. To donate via FOCA, please see thie information below. 

If you are interested in supporting our team with a general tax-deductible donation, please mail your check to : 

CRLS Crew 
P.O. Box 400116 
Cambridge, MA 02140 

Please make checks payable to “FOCA/CRLS Crew.” Thank you for your support!

A receipt will be mailed to your address. CRLS Crew is a non-profit organization under the umbrella of the Friends of Cambridge Athletics.

All general donations are tax deductible in accordance with Federal 501c3 regulations. Employer Identification Number (EIN): 04-3480847

### Team Awards

*<https://crlsrowing.org/trophycase/teamawards/>*

CRLS Crew has achieved tremendous success as a competitive elite rowing program. In the process of building fast, strong, intimidating crews and holding our team to the highest standards as student-athletes, we seek to recognize those additional qualities we value highly as a community, which make our team strong. The awards below are given to honor those student-athletes who go above and beyond the common.

Bill Becklean Award 

The Bill Becklean Award is given out to the rare athlete. The award therefore is not given out every year, but rather only when an athlete so greatly exemplifies the values of the team and the spirit of Coach Bill Becklean that its awarding is warranted.

This athlete consistently demonstrates commitment to the team by showing up on time, prepared, with a positive attitude and an eager mindset. Their passion for the sport is seen not only through their excellence as an individual, but through their ability to inspire excellence in those around them. They are compassionate. They are dedicated. They are selfless. They embody all of the qualities and characteristics that make Coach Bill Becklean an invaluable member of our community.

Coach’s Award 

The Coach’s Award is given to the athlete who, on a daily basis, helps the team run seamlessly. Their attitude and effort are key to establishing a team culture that positively reflects the values of CRLS Crew. Their selflessness, leadership, and eagerness to take initiative allows the coaching staff to work, without having to work harder to get the team on board.

| 2015-2016

| Isabella Gray (’16) and Alex Shulman (’16)

| 2014-2015

| Giulia Johnson (’17) and Jake Steinberg (’17)

| 2013-2014

| Anna Jeffers (’15) and Ben Schooler (’14)

Falcon Award 

The Falcon Award is about athleticism. It’s about grit. It’s about spirit. Passion. Commitment. The Falcon Award is given to the most dedicated athlete on the team. The athlete who goes above and beyond what could ever be expected of them, with no expectation of anything in return.

| 2015-2016

| Bella Jaffe (’17) and Sepehr Khansalar (’16)

| 2014-2015

| Phoebe Hyland (’17) and Yuri Goto (’15)

| 2013-2014

| Noa Randall (’14) and Nick Clover (’14)

The Hammer 

The Hammer Award is typically given to the athlete with the fastest erg score. It’s awarding can also be determined at the coach’s discretion and will always be given to the athlete who demonstrates the greatest effort and accomplishment toward physical excellence.

Tough As Nails Award 

The Tough As Nails Award is given to the novice athlete who faces any challenge the team throws their way with a positive attitude and a smile on their face. They’re committed to constant improvement and contributing to the team any chance they get.
